Round-4 review of the third codex revision of the design for issue #4525 (positron-ai/tron)

Did the third revision close the round-3 findings, and is the typed KV-cache tensor design implementable now?

Reviewed 2026-09-22 UTC by Claude (Fable 5.1) for jhan. Design reviewed: status/design-new-tensor-type.html, third revision by codex after the round-3 review (finalized 2026-09-22 16:43 UTC, 168,971 bytes, sha256 d2e9dabf). Sources pinned to main f46e48ba (the design's snapshot), main 2880c3aa9b (the tip the design's S15-S21 cite), the live main tip 0a51385e95 (fetched 2026-09-22 17:05 UTC; one inserted line in model.hpp since 2880c3aa9b, nothing else among the design's files) and PR 4424 head 30c4ac82cb.
Short version

The third revision resolved 71 of the 79 open items (52 round-3 findings plus 27 residuals from earlier rounds). 8 are partly resolved, each a one-sentence wording gap; none is unresolved and none regressed.

This round found 47 new defects: 1 major, 11 minor, 35 notes. The major one is a process gap, not a design error: the design demands proof that the required Test host job executed the new tests before a test is committed, a draft pull request runs no Test host job, and the page names no route to that evidence (J01). One new sentence on the production write path is wrong (the model's V buffer carries dma = true, not false; J09); it cannot produce wrong bytes, but it must change.

Verdict: accept with minor edits, implementable now. All three judges say so; two ask for the J01 paragraph before the first test commit. After that paragraph and the one-sentence edits listed in section 7, another agent can build the parent from this page.

1. Words used here

tron
the inference program under test; its source is the positron-ai/tron repository
PR 4424, the child
the open draft pull request "VNNI K: store the K cache of 128-dimension heads in the AMX VNNI layout, with a shared block save" (branch jhan-amx-vnniK, head 30c4ac82cb). After the new parent PR exists, PR 4424 is rebased onto it and is then called the child
the parent PR
the new pull request that issue #4525 asks for: typed access to the existing V and K caches on main, with no new layout
Ben, the reviewer
the tron maintainer who reviewed PR 4424 on 2026-09-21 (a worker-index question, a review asking for typed tensors, and an API sketch). The design calls him "the reviewer" and, since this revision, no longer names him
issue #4525
the GitHub issue codex created for the parent PR; jhan edited its title and first sentence on 2026-09-22 00:10 UTC; unchanged since
KV cache, K, V
the saved key (K) and value (V) vectors of earlier tokens; attention reads them
page, slot, head, D
a page holds 64 token positions; a slot is the cache region of one layer (or of several layers that share it); a head is one attention head; D is the head size, the number of values per token per head (128 for PR 4424's models)
bf16
the 16-bit brain floating-point format used for cached K and V
AMX, AVX-512, 16-lane and 8-lane builds
AMX and AVX-512 are CPU instruction sets. tron's vector width TRON_CHUNK_SIZE is 16 fp32 values with AVX-512 (the 16-lane build) and 8 without (the 8-lane build); the 8-lane build stores V row-major, so it has no pair-interleaved V
packed V (pair-interleaved V)
today's V storage in the 16-lane build: for each dimension, tokens 2p and 2p+1 sit next to each other. Element offset = (token/2)*(2*D) + 2*dim + token%2
native K
today's K storage: one token's D values consecutive in memory (row-major)
blocked packed K (VNNI K)
PR 4424's K storage for 128-dimension heads: two consecutive dimensions of one token share a 4-byte unit, and 16 tokens share one 64-byte line; a 16-token by 32-dimension panel is one AMX tile of 1,024 bytes
expr, view, const_view, tensor
tron's expression interface (h/tron/kernels/expr.hpp), its typed pointer views over regularly laid-out memory and their read-only form (h/tron/tensor/view.hpp, views.hpp), and its owning arrays (h/tron/tensor/tensor.hpp)
kv_block
the struct that holds K then V for one head of one slot in one page (h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp)
set_v, get_v, append_v, scaled_v, v_data, v_base
today's V functions in kv_cache.hpp: write one token, read one token, copy one token between pages, compute the weighted V sum, and return the raw pointer of a V plane (the last two are removed by the design)
scaled_v_expr
detail::scaled_v_expr, the expression in kv_cache.hpp that computes the weighted V sum; its 16-lane constructor loads whole 64-byte pair tiles with _mm512_load_si512 and multiplies them with _mm512_dpbf16_ps
save_v_impl
the model function (h/tron/models/model.hpp) that stores one token's V into the cache during a forward pass; it calls page::set_v
Zero-Initialized V Slots invariant
the rule in kv_cache.hpp (Note [Zero-Initialized V Slots], lines 2055-2065) that every even-token V write zeroes its odd partner row. The weighted sum reads a whole pair even when the token count is odd, and NaN times 0 is NaN, so the padding row must be zero
arena, book
an arena is one allocated memory region holding cache blocks; book is the class that owns the arenas and the pages; kv_blocks_alias is the base pointer the uniform-reclaimability accessor indexes across slots
EAGLE
the speculative model; its cache adds one extra physical KV slot at the end of the retained arena and a separate embedding buffer (x_data) that holds no kv_block; it requires uniform geometry
GOF, page_info
group of four: four tokens of K and V staged for the FPGA's hardware KV cache; the staging code reaches a page's rows through the page_info callbacks in h/tron/gof.hpp (namespace tron::hardware); the V callback (v_head_fn in h/tron/scheduler/full.hpp) unpacks one row into caller-owned scratch
CI, Nix test build, TRON_AMX_DISPATCH, TRON_K_VNNI, TRON_AMX_DISABLE
CI is the automated build and test. The Nix test build is the required CI lane (workflow gcp-nix.yml). TRON_AMX_DISPATCH and TRON_K_VNNI are CMake options that compile the AMX attention kernels and PR 4424's K layout; the Nix lane sets neither. TRON_AMX_DISABLE=1 is an environment variable that makes an AMX build take the non-AMX path; it is read once per process. Issue #3997 tracks the AMX gap
Slice, logs-<host>/run-NNNN, test-cost records
bin/slice is the repository's test scheduler. It writes one log per test binary under <checkout>/logs-<runner hostname>/run-NNNN and keeps at most 10 such directories. config/test-benchmarks.json holds one duration and memory record per test and platform block (genoa96, genoa32, granite_rapids_6960p, granite_rapids_6962p); bin/slice bench --update or --new refreshes them, bin/slice bench --check only reports missing records
lint-notes, lint-toolchain, Note [Name]
make lint-notes runs the checker for the repository's documentation Notes (a Note [Name] heading underlined with ~~~, referenced as See Note [Name]). The required Lint job builds the Nix check lint-toolchain, which ends with make lint-notes
the generator, TronCpp.hs, LoopyTronSpec.hs
ingest/src/TronCpp.hs is the Haskell program that emits the model C++ (run() for the main thread, run_main_help for helper threads); ingest/test/LoopyTronSpec.hs asserts the generated call text; bin/ingest-cabal test runs those tests and the required Test ingest job runs them in CI
the marker
the file /bill-has-instance-0,2 on delphi-3bda; when present, another user may use the first half of the machine (tron --instance 0,2); jhan's harness always runs on the second half and never changes it; exec/bill-share.sh take/release removes and restores it
codex, the design, the third revision
codex is the agent jhan asked to answer Ben; the design is the page it wrote, status/design-new-tensor-type.html; the third revision is the version finalized 2026-09-22 16:43 UTC (168,971 bytes) after the round-3 review. It is the version reviewed here
round 1, F.. and C.. ids
the first Claude review of the original design (2026-09-22 ~03:00 UTC), archived as claude-review-design-new-tensor-type-r1.html. Its 58 retained findings are F01-F59 (finders) and C01-C09 (completeness critic)
round 2, N.. and M.. ids
the second Claude review, of the first revision (2026-09-22 ~06:20 UTC), archived as claude-review-design-new-tensor-type-r2.html. It graded the 58 round-1 findings (35 resolved, 20 partly, 3 deferred) and added 64 findings N01-N62 (finders) and M01-M08 (critic)
round 3, G.. and H.. ids
the third Claude review, of the second revision (2026-09-22 ~09:30 UTC), archived as claude-review-design-new-tensor-type-r3.html. It graded the 87 open items (60 resolved, 25 partly, 2 deferred) and added 52 findings G01-G51 (finders; G11 and G43 rejected, G52 dropped) and H01-H03 (critic; H04 dropped). The design cites these ids in brackets, for example [G01]
round 4, J.. and L.. ids, the 79 open items
this review. J.. are new findings from the ten finders, L.. from the completeness critic. The 79 open items are the 52 round-3 findings plus the 27 items round 3 graded partly or deferred; section 3 grades each against the third revision
v_vnni_tensor, v_vnni_view, v_vnni_row, detail::v_vnni_access
the design's names for the packed-V owner (the aligned array inside the cache block), the typed rank-2 view over it, its logical row expression, and the helper in h/tron/tensor/v_vnni.hpp that alone may return the packed pointer; the design permits typed V operations, weights_times_v_128x4 and scaled_v_expr to use it
append_v_row, load_row, copy_token
the design's typed bulk operations: write one token's V row into the packed plane (an even token zeroes its odd partner), read one row out, copy one token between planes. append_v_row is the design's name for the sketch's V store_row. The design adds no copy_plane for V
kv_cache_fwd.hpp, v_vnni.hpp, k_vnni.hpp
the design's new headers under h/tron/tensor/: a declarations-only header (no intrinsics) that amx_attn_iface.hpp includes, the V definitions header, and the child's K definitions header (the child's existing h/tron/kernels/k_vnni.hpp keeps only qk_group)
native_k_view, const_native_k_view
the design's new aliases for today's row-major K view types; const_native_k_view<64, 128> becomes the K parameter of qk_rowmajor_128x4
page::v_packed, page::k_packed
the design's read-only accessors returning the packed V view (parent, 16-lane build only) and the packed K view (child)
t_amx_numerics, bf16_rne, qk_inputs
the test t/t_amx_numerics.cpp that compares the real AMX kernels with scalar references; bf16_rne is its round-to-nearest-even helper; qk_inputs is its 64-byte-aligned K and Q fixture struct; its PV case builds the packed V page by hand in a std::vector today
Q1 to Q4, D5, the decision record
design section 2's four questions for Ben (address formula in the view; const at both ranks; logical rows through operator[]; the smaller parent scope) with a proposed choice, a fallback and a record status, plus D5, the native-K typing scope jhan requested; the record is copied into the parent PR description
resolved / partly / deferred / unresolved / regressed
the five disposition statuses of section 3; their meanings are given above the table
blocker / major / minor / note
the four severities of section 4; their meanings are given above the table
source, design-text and intent lenses
the three verifier roles: one checks every fact against the source at the pinned commit, one reads the whole design for fairness, one weighs whether the defect matters for an implementable parent that Ben will accept
draft gating, Run CI, ready_for_review
in the required GCP Nix workflow the product jobs (Build ingest, Build model traces, Prepare Tron build inputs, and through them Build Tron, Test host, Test FPGA and Test) run for a pull request only when it is not a draft or carries the Run CI label; a draft pull request runs only the Lint, CI machinery and Fresh Cabal plan jobs. ready_for_review is the GitHub event that fires when a draft is promoted; it is one of the workflow's pull_request triggers
dmatensor, btensor, host executor
dmatensor is tron's owning array in DMA-safe memory (h/tron/hardware/dmatensor.hpp); its views carry dma = true. hardware::btensor is the bf16 dmatensor the tp1, tp2 and tp4 executors use for the model's V buffer. The host executor uses a plain tensor whose views carry dma = false; t_llama_unit's model cases use that executor

2. What changed between the round-3 and round-4 versions of the design

The third revision keeps the ten-section structure and grows the page by about 39 percent (121,774 to 168,971 bytes; 11,746 to 15,133 words). Every non-ASCII glyph stays a numeric entity. Codex again changed no production code, posted nothing to GitHub, and edited no issue; the header, section 9 and section 10 say so.

The table lists the changes an implementer would notice. The word-level diff (210 changed segments, 1,474 words deleted and 4,861 inserted) is saved as evidence/claude-review-r4/design-r3-to-r4-wdiff.txt.

3. Did the revision address the 79 open items?

Each of the 79 open items (the 52 round-3 findings G01-G51 without G11 and G43, H01-H03, and the 27 items round 3 graded partly or deferred) was checked against the third revision by one checker agent and then independently re-graded by two adversarial re-graders (a source-truth lens and a fairness lens). The status below is the majority of the three; where I (the lead) changed a status after reading the votes, the row says so.

Findings in full

Major J01. Draft parent runs no Test host job; the required execution evidence has no stated route

Where
8. CI ownership and execution evidence (design-r4.txt:494-497); 3. Add the tensor interface to main first (:167); 8. Build configurations (:485); Definition of done (:535-536)
Earlier ids
touches N36 F16
Claim
The GCP Nix product pipeline does not run for a draft pull request unless it carries the Run CI label. The jobs Build ingest, Build model traces and Prepare Tron build inputs each carry the condition github.event_name != 'pull_request' || !github.event.pull_request.draft || contains(github.event.pull_request.labels.*.name, 'Run CI'). Build Tron needs Prepare Tron build inputs, and Test host needs Build Tron. So a draft parent without Run CI has no Test host job at all. The design requires required-suite execution evidence before committing a new test (:495). It forbids the Run CI label without user direction (:167, :495). It places gates 'before leaving draft' (:485, :536) and makes 'Required tests collect and execute new behavior coverage with the named evidence' a completion condition (:535). It never states when that evidence can exist. It can exist only after the parent is marked ready for review (the pull_request trigger includes ready_for_review, and the stack rule permits ready-for-review for the PR based on main) or after a user-directed Run CI label. The design also names no local way to verify 'collection' before that point. An implementing agent following :495 on a draft parent cannot obtain the evidence and is told not to use the only label that would produce it. Rounds 1-3 (F16, N36) covered passage-marker reuse, not the draft filter.
Design says
Before committing a new test, satisfy the repository rule: verify required-suite collection and execution, or obtain the user's explicit exception. Local success alone is insufficient. Do not add a dedicated CI job or a Run CI label to bypass this rule.
Evidence
.github/workflows/gcp-nix.yml@2880c3aa9b:3-9 (Note [Draft PR Filtering and Label Override]: 'Draft PRs run lint, machinery, and fresh-plan checks; Run CI enables the product pipeline.'), :14 (pull_request types: opened, synchronize, reopened, ready_for_review), :168-171 (build-ingest if:), :294-297 (build-model-traces if:), :382-385 (prepare-tron-build-inputs if:), :443 (tron-build needs: [prepare-tron-build-inputs, wait-for-lab-cache]), :534 (test-host needs: tron-build), :538 (RUN_TESTS applies only inside an existing Test host job). AGENTS.md@2880c3aa9b:12-18 (only the PR based on main may be ready for review; no Run CI without user direction), :22-28 (verify collection and execution before committing; agents may not decide an exception). Collection is checkable locally: nix/tron-test-environment.nix@2880c3aa9b:31-34 (tron-test-host = bin/slice run --filter=host), t/CMakeLists.txt@2880c3aa9b:586-592 (t_llama_unit registered with LABELS fake), .agents/skills/run-tron-tests/SKILL.md@2880c3aa9b:61-62 (make list-tests). design-r4.txt:167, :485, :495-497, :535-536. r2-findings.md:487-505 (N36 covers marker reuse only). Merge note: single candidate; severity major kept as graded.
Correction from verification
Three narrowings. (1) Citation: design-r4.txt:485 is a blank line and the build-configuration table (:474-482) says nothing about draft state; the only "Before leaving draft" sentence is :536. Cite ":536" alone for that gate and drop ":485" from the section list. (2) The sub-claim "The design also names no local way to verify 'collection'" overstates. The design does name make test-host at :514. That target runs bin/slice run --filter=host --exclude-tag=slow [GNUmakefile@2880c3aa9b:905-906], the same host filter the CI runner uses [nix/tron-test-environment.nix@2880c3aa9b:31-34] minus slow-tagged tests. Reword to: "The design names make test-host (:514) but does not say that it applies the CI runner's host filter, and :495 declares local success insufficient, so the implementer is not told that a local host-filter run or make list-tests is the collection check." (3) The recommendation's sentence "Required execution evidence for the parent therefore exists only after the parent is marked ready for review ... or after a user-directed Run CI label" should add the third route the workflow allows: a user-directed gh workflow run gcp-nix.yml --ref <parent-branch> also passes the filter because github.event_name != 'pull_request' [gcp-nix.yml@2880c3aa9b:17-20, 168-169], but the design's own rule at :498 counts manual dispatch results as separate evidence, not required PR coverage, so name it only to exclude it. Keep the rest of the recommendation as written; every command and line it cites is verified above. Add the live evidence to the finding's evidence list: PR 4424 run 35046296975 (Lint success, Test host skipped) versus PR 4505 run 35552493475 (Test host ran).
Narrow three points. (1) The claim "It can exist only after ready-for-review or a Run CI label" omits a third mechanism: gcp-nix.yml has a workflow_dispatch trigger (gcp-nix.yml@2880c3aa9b:17-22), and the product jobs' if: passes when github.event_name != 'pull_request' (:168-171), so a user-directed gh workflow run gcp-nix.yml --ref <parent-branch> produces a Test host run at the same source. That run is not attached to the PR as a required check, and the design's own rule at design-r4.txt:497 treats manual dispatch results as "separate evidence, not required PR coverage". Reword to: "Required execution evidence attached to the PR exists only after ready-for-review or a user-directed Run CI label; a user-directed dispatch of gcp-nix.yml proves collection and execution at the same source but is separate evidence under :497." (2) The claim "The design also names no local way to verify collection" is too strong. The design names make test-host (design-r4.txt:514) and links the run-tron-tests skill (:512), whose test-registration entry lists make list-tests (.agents/skills/run-tron-tests/SKILL.md@2880c3aa9b:61-62). make test-host runs bin/slice run --filter=host --exclude-tag=slow (GNUmakefile@2880c3aa9b:905-906), the same filter as the required tron-test-host runner minus slow-tagged tests (nix/tron-test-environment.nix@2880c3aa9b:31-34). Reword to: "The design does not connect make test-host or make list-tests to the AGENTS.md collection check, and does not say that CI execution evidence cannot exist while the parent is draft." (3) Add one precision to the recommendation: a GitHub rerun of a draft run does not create the Test host job either, because the job if: is re-evaluated against the same draft event payload and label changes take effect only on the next PR update (gcp-nix.yml@2880c3aa9b:6-7). So the sentence at design-r4.txt:496 "A user-directed GitHub rerun also executes the tests" holds only once the Test host job exists. The rest of the recommendation stands: name the draft gate after :495, state the ready-for-review route as permitted by the stack rule (AGENTS.md:12-13), change :536 to "Before marking the parent ready for review", and tie :535 to the first GCP Nix run after ready-for-review or a user-directed Run CI run.
Narrow the finding in three places and fix one citation. (a) Section list and claim: drop :485 and :536 from the conflict. Those gates are local (16-lane AMX-on compile plus fake dispatch cases, which required CI never builds [design-r4.txt:492]); keep :167, :494-497 and :535. (b) Replace "cannot obtain the evidence and is told not to use the only label that would produce it" with: "the design does not say that the product jobs are draft-gated, so it does not say that the evidence exists only after the parent is marked ready for review (permitted by :167 and triggered by ready_for_review, gcp-nix.yml@2880c3aa9b:14), after a user-directed rerun of such a run (:497), or after a user-directed Run CI label; an agent may stall or ask for an unneeded label or exception." (c) Recommendation: keep the one added sentence after :495 (draft filter, ready-for-review route, citations gcp-nix.yml@2880c3aa9b:3-9, 14, 168-171, 294-297, 382-385, 443, 534-536, 599-601). For the local collection check, cite bin/slice@2880c3aa9b:1398 and :3106 (--filter=host selects every registered test whose labels do not include fpga), together with t/CMakeLists.txt@2880c3aa9b:586-592 (t_llama_unit, LABELS fake) and nix/tron-test-environment.nix@2880c3aa9b:31-34 (tron-test-host runs bin/slice run --filter=host); the commands make list-tests [GNUmakefile@2880c3aa9b:972-974] and bin/slice build-targets --build-dir=gen --filter=host [GNUmakefile@2880c3aa9b:360] both exist. Drop the change to :536; "leaving draft" and "marking ready for review" mean the same thing there, and that bullet is a local gate. For :535 add "from a GCP Nix run whose Test host steps executed: the first run after ready-for-review, a user-directed rerun, or a user-directed Run CI run", not "the first run", because :497 already covers marker reuse and reruns. (d) Grade minor: one sentence of text should change; the route is already permitted at :167 and the evidence fields at :496 already prevent a false record.
Recommendation
Add to the CI section after :495: 'The GCP Nix product jobs (Build ingest, Build model traces, Prepare Tron build inputs, and therefore Build Tron, Test host, Test FPGA and Test) do not run for a draft pull request unless it carries the Run CI label [gcp-nix.yml@2880c3aa9b:3-9, 14, 168-171, 294-297, 382-385, 443, 534-536]. Do not add that label without user direction [AGENTS.md:17]. Collection is checked before the first test commit: the new cases live in t_llama_unit, which t/CMakeLists.txt:586-592 registers with LABELS fake and the Test host runner executes through bin/slice run --filter=host [nix/tron-test-environment.nix:31-34]; make test-host applies the same host filter locally, and make list-tests or bin/slice build-targets --build-dir=gen --filter=host lists the collected binaries. Execution evidence attached to the PR exists only after one of three user-directed actions: promoting the parent to ready for review (the stack rule allows this for the PR based on main [AGENTS.md:12-13], and ready_for_review runs the product pipeline), a user-directed Run CI label, or a user-directed rerun. A user-directed gh workflow run gcp-nix.yml --ref <parent-branch> proves collection and execution at the same source but is separate evidence under :497. Complete the 16-lane AMX-on local gate (:480) first, then ask for the promotion; record the run URL and attempt under :496. The Test host runner is normally not delphi-3bda (J36), so plan for the :504 fallback: the per-binary pass of t_llama_unit at that run URL is the execution evidence when the runner is unreachable, and an exception request is needed only if a reused passage marker skips the run and a rerun is refused.' Rewrite :504's last sentences to match.
Lead's reading
Holds 3 of 3 (source and intent at minor, design-text at major); the implementer and risk judges list it as the one must-fix. The lead keeps major: the design's own rule at :495 cannot be met on a draft pull request, the page never says so, and an implementer either stalls or asks for the label the design forbids. Corrections folded into the recommendation: :485 is not a gate (only :536 says before leaving draft); the design does name make test-host (:514), which applies the CI runner's host filter, so a collection check exists but is not called one; a user-directed gh workflow run gcp-nix.yml --ref <branch> is a third route, which :497 classes as separate evidence. J36 adds the measurement that the Test host runner is normally not delphi-3bda, so the log fallback at :504 is the usual case, not the exception.
Found by
process-ci; 3 of 3 verifiers could not refute it (votes and corrections)

Minor J02. Terms used without definition: B tile, Delphi/Andoria, worktree, sw-dev-01, GOF credits, guard words, descriptor pair

Where
1. Words used here (design-r4.txt:23-62); uses at :222, :447, :449, :476, :482, :504, :513, :516-518
Earlier ids
touches G02 F23
Claim
Two terms are new in this revision and undefined: 'AMX B tile' (:222) and 'another Delphi or Andoria host' (:504). Five carried terms are also undefined: 'worktree' (:476, :482), 'sw-dev-01' (:513), 'GOF credits' (:449), and the host-guard words 'campaign', 'blackout' and 'instance' (:516-518). :447 names 'the operation-index pair and the descriptor pair' of save_k overloads without saying what a descriptor is. The Words table defines 'AMX tile' implicitly (:49) but not what 'B' means. The B-tile statement itself is right: the child's cache comment says the AMX QK kernel consumes the packed plane as its B operand. The project rule asks for a definition at first use of code names, machine names and jargon. These terms fail that rule.
Evidence
Design quote and evidence
Each 16-token by 32-dimension panel is one AMX B tile.
design-r4.txt:23-62 (no row for any of the terms); :222 (B tile), :447 (descriptor pair), :449 (GOF credits), :476 and :482 (worktree), :504 (Delphi or Andoria), :513 (sw-dev-01, delphi-3bda), :516 (campaign, blackout), :517 (blackout), :518 (instance). design-wdiff.txt:134-135 (B tile sentence new in round 4), :436-437 (Delphi/Andoria sentence new in round 4). h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp@30c4ac82cb:2421 ('consumes this layout directly as its B operand') and :1683-1684. h/tron/models/model.hpp@30c4ac82cb:2952-2962 (save_k_helper pair, one templated on operation_ix) and :3065-3073 (save_k pair, one taking attention_operation_descriptor const&). design-r3.txt:476 (sw-dev-01 sentence unchanged since round 3). Merge note: single candidate, severity minor kept. The original recommendation's clause 'not a repository rule' about sw-dev-01 was removed here, because rejected round-1 candidate F18 already lost that claim (r1-findings.md:637-646: jhan's codex instruction file names sw-dev-01 as the build host). The finding asks only for a definition.

Minor J03. pair_base names two different functions; the Words row defines only the V meaning

Where
1. Words used here (design-r4.txt:49); 5. Operation table, row.chunk(i) row (:257); 7. Then adapt PR #4424, header split bullet (:416)
Earlier ids
touches G17 G48
Claim
The Words row defines pair_base as the address of the first bf16 value of a V token pair. That matches the V formula at :257 (pair_base = plane + (token / 2) * 2 * D, in bf16 elements). The sentence added in round 4 at :416 tells the implementer to move 'their pair_base, pair_row_offsets, and transpose_16x16_epi32 helpers' into h/tron/tensor/k_vnni.hpp. That pair_base is the child's detail::pair_base(plane, s, token). It returns a char* byte address: the 4-byte lane of dimension pair 0 of dimension group s for one token inside a packed-K panel. The panel's 16 pair rows follow at 64-byte steps. The two meanings differ in unit (bf16 elements versus bytes), in layout (V pair block versus K panel lane) and in arguments. The Words table gives only the V meaning, so a reader applies the V definition to the K helper. The K use is new in this revision; the Words row is unchanged from round 3.
Evidence
Design quote and evidence
pair_base is the address of the first bf16 value of a V token pair. [...] Move their pair_base, pair_row_offsets, and transpose_16x16_epi32 helpers with them.
design-r4.txt:49 (Words row 'bf16s, panel, pair_base'), :257 (V formula), :416 (K helper move). h/tron/kernels/k_vnni.hpp@30c4ac82cb:109-124: comment 'The byte address of dimension pair 0 of dimension step s for token token. The pair rows dp = 0..15 of that panel follow at 64-byte steps'; char* pair_base(bf16* plane, size_t s, size_t token) at :113 and the const overload at :119, returning reinterpret_cast<char*>(plane) + ((s * TOKEN_BLOCKS_4 + c) * PANEL_DWORDS_256 + t) * DWORD_BYTES_4. design-wdiff.txt:376-377 (the :416 sentence is inserted in round 4). design-r3.txt:49 (identical Words row in round 3); design-r3.txt has pair_base only at :49 and :246 (no K use). Merge note: three candidates (two minor, one note); minor kept because both an implementer and a reader of the formula are affected.
Correction from verification
Two small narrowings. (1) The child's comment says "dimension step s", not "dimension group s" [k_vnni.hpp@30c4ac82cb:110]. The design's own layout text says "dimension groups" and "s = dimension / 32" [design-r4.txt:222]. The recommended Words text should use the design's word and give the child's in parentheses: "dimension group s (the child's comment says dimension step)". (2) The recommended citation "k_vnni.hpp:109-180" also spans scatter_row (:135-146), which is not one of the three helpers. Cite the two detail blocks instead: "k_vnni.hpp@30c4ac82cb:109-133 (pair_base at :113 and :119, pair_row_offsets at :126) and :148-180 (transpose_16x16_epi32 at :153)". The rest of the recommendation stands.
Three narrowings. (1) Lower the implementation risk in the claim: write "An implementer is not at risk of moving or writing the wrong code, because :416 says 'Move their ... helpers', which names existing child functions, and the parent defines no pair_base function. The defect is that the Words row [design-r4.txt:49] asserts a single meaning that the design's own :416 contradicts, and that a reader who looks up the :416 term gets the V definition." (2) Fix the line range in the recommendation: the child's detail helpers sit in two blocks, :109-133 (pair_base at :113 and :119, pair_row_offsets at :126) and :148-180 (transpose_16x16_epi32 at :153); scatter_row at :139-146 lies between them and is not a detail helper [k_vnni.hpp@30c4ac82cb]. Write "(k_vnni.hpp:109-133 and :148-180)" instead of "(k_vnni.hpp:109-180)". (3) Offer the smaller fix as an alternative: instead of a two-meaning Words row, rename the V formula variable at :257 (for example "pair_start") and update :49 to match, or write "the child's detail::pair_base" at :416. The round-3 row said the K access helper follows the same rule as the V helper: cache callers and tests do not use it. The round-4 rewrite of that row names permitted users for detail::v_vnni_access only (typed V operations, weights_times_v_128x4, detail::scaled_v_expr) and ends with a bare sentence that the child defines detail::k_vnni_access. No sentence in the round-4 design says who may call k_vnni_access or that page methods, book and tests may not (grep of design-r4.txt: k_vnni_access appears only at :260 and :416). The child needs that rule. The design makes qk_group (the child's AVX-512 query/key score reader in h/tron/kernels/k_vnni.hpp) and qk_vnni_128x4 (the AMX reader in src/tron/kernels/amx_attn.cpp) take k_vnni_view<const bf16,64,128> by value (:434). Both read the plane through a raw pointer today, so both must become permitted users, while page::k_packed (:431) and t_k_vnni_layout must not. :434 also says 'the corresponding shape to qk_group' without naming the type; qk_group reads one whole 64-token by 128-dimension plane, so the same view type applies. Only the layout test is covered elsewhere (:443 'avoid the internal friend helpers'). Two smaller gaps in the same bullets: :417 names self_attention.hpp as the only file that includes the kernel header directly for qk_group, but t/t_k_vnni_layout.cpp also calls qk_group directly and needs that include after the header split. This is the K analog of round-3 G01 (V helper users) and a regression of the round-3 G17 wording.
Evidence

[...] self_attention.hpp includes the kernel header directly for qk_group'); :434; :428 ('private packed-pointer rules to K too'); :443; :533 (parent-only wording 'layout-specific kernels'). Child sources: h/tron/kernels/k_vnni.hpp@30c4ac82cb:247-252 template <size_t kv_mul, typename q_scalar> void qk_group(const bf16* plane, const q_scalar* q, uint64_t live, float* s, size_t s_stride), :260 recursive call with plane, :273 const bf16* panel = plane + (step * TOKEN_BLOCKS_4 + c) * PANEL_ELEMS_512;, :278 _mm512_maskz_loadu_epi32(mask, panel + dp * ROW_ELEMS_32) (loops over all TOKEN_BLOCKS_4 and DIM_STEPS_4, so the argument is one whole plane). h/tron/kernels/amx_attn_iface.hpp@30c4ac82cb:251 void qk_vnni_128x4(const bf16* k_vnni_plane, ...); src/tron/kernels/amx_attn.cpp@30c4ac82cb:201, :241-250 _tile_loadd(K_TILE_5, k_vnni_plane + (step * TOKEN_BLOCKS_4 + 0) * PANEL_ELEMS_512, ...). h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp@30c4ac82cb:1689 (k_vnni_plane returns const bf16*). Minor J09. Model V buffer as_view() carries dma = true in tp1/tp2/tp4 executors, not false

Where
5. Interface design, row-write rule (design-r4.txt:270); 7. step 3 Model save (:391); 9. rows :573 and :609
Earlier ids
touches G41
Claim
The new sentence states a wrong DMA-flag value for the production V save buffer. The buffer passed to save_v is intermediate_t<n_kv_heads * head_size> vouts. intermediate_t is indexed_vector<token_job_id, btensor_t<width>>, and btensor_t is plugin_t::btensor_t. make_llm_executor defaults BTensor to hardware::btensor, which tp1/tp2/tp4 use, and hardware::btensor is dmatensor<bf16, ...> whose as_view() returns tron::view<Scalar, true, true, seq<N>, sseq<1>> with static constexpr bool dma = true. So vs[ix].as_view() carries dma = true in every production executor. Only the host executor (make_llm_executor<false, tensor, tensor, tensor>) yields dma = false. An implementer who follows the sentence literally and wraps &vs[ix][kv_head * head_size] in an explicit const_view<bf16, true, false, seq<D>, sseq<1>> would compile and run (no packed body reads the flag, :252) but would discard the buffer's DMA-safe flag, contradicting 'Preserve existing DMA flags' (:573, :609). The round-3 text stated no value, so this error is new in round 4. This candidate contradicts facts-r4.md:168-170 ('The buffer type has as_view() with dma = false (model.hpp:1381-1383 per round-3 G41)'), with its own source chain; the verifier must settle which buffer type model.hpp:1381-1383 describes.
Evidence
Design quote and evidence
The model source already has a view with dma = false.
h/tron/plugins/llama.hpp@2880c3aa9b:547 (intermediate_t<n_kv_heads * head_size> vouts;), :1045-1046 (call to save_v). h/tron/models/model.hpp@2880c3aa9b:736 (intermediate_t = indexed_vector<token_job_id, btensor_t<width>>), :733 (btensor_t = plugin_t::btensor_t). h/tron/models/common.hpp@2880c3aa9b:79 (make_llm_executor defaults BTensor = hardware::btensor), :94-96 (tp1/tp2/tp4), :100 (host = make_llm_executor<false, tensor, tensor, tensor>). h/tron/hardware/btensor.hpp@2880c3aa9b:21 (using btensor = dmatensor<bf16, i, is...>). h/tron/hardware/dmatensor.hpp@2880c3aa9b:38 (static constexpr bool dma = true), :223-224 (tron::view<Scalar, true, true, seq<N>, sseq<1>> as_view() noexcept). h/tron/tensor/tensor.hpp@2880c3aa9b:35, :71-77 (host tensor dma = false). design-r4.txt:252 (no packed body reads the flag), :270, :391, :573, :609. facts-r4.md:167-170 (the lead's contrary note citing model.hpp:1381-1383). Merge note: single candidate, minor kept; flagged as contradicting the facts file with evidence, so it needs primary-source verification, not a drop.
Correction from verification
Three narrowings. (1) Citation: the rank-1 dmatensor's static constexpr bool dma = true is at h/tron/hardware/dmatensor.hpp@2880c3aa9b:173; line :38 is the rank-2-and-up inductive case (also true). vouts elements are rank-1 btensor_t<width>, so cite :173. (2) Wording "in every production executor": say "tp1, tp2, tp4 and perm_tp1/perm_tp2/perm_tp4 (the ingest default is tp1 [ingest/build-model.py@2880c3aa9b:295; ingest/README.md:68, :140]); host_bf16 and host_fp16 also carry true [common.hpp:101-102]; only host (float tensor) carries false [common.hpp:100; tensor.hpp:35]". Note that host is a valid ingest executor value [ingest/build-model.py:15] and is the executor the t_llama_unit model cases use [t/t_llama_unit.cpp@2880c3aa9b:1252, :1298, :1323, :1852, :1986], so dma = false is right for those test instantiations and wrong for the production ones. (3) Add to the claim: facts-r4.md:168 cites model.hpp:1381-1383, which is the generic attention_buffer_width size helper and says nothing about the flag; the "dma = false" value came from the round-3 G41 LEAD NOTE [r3-findings.md:747] against two round-3 verifier corrections [r3-findings.md:748, :750]. Recommendation: keep the proposed replacement sentence, and optionally name the concrete form the 8-lane branch already uses so Dma is deduced rather than typed: build the source from slice<geometry.kv.head_size>()(vs[ix].as_view(), kv_head * geometry.kv.head_size) [model.hpp@2880c3aa9b:2836-2838], which keeps the buffer's aligned and dma flags [slice.hpp:53-78], then convert explicitly to const_view as :270 already requires.

Minor J10. Access-helper call rule has no check; the rg audit patterns omit the helper names

Where
5. Interface design, Internal storage access row (design-r4.txt:260); 7. Implementation steps, fixture-migration searches (:405-407)
Earlier ids
touches G21 M07
Claim
The design forbids page methods, book, and tests from calling detail::v_vnni_access (and by parallel detail::k_vnni_access). Neither search in section 7 can detect a violation. The first rg pattern lists eleven names and not the helpers. The second search looks only for reinterpret_cast in two headers. A call to the helper returns the pointer the view already stores, so it needs no cast and escapes both searches. The implementer cannot perform the completion check the rule implies.
Evidence
Design quote and evidence
Page methods, book , and tests do not call it. [...] rg -n "\b(v_base|v_data|set_v|get_v|append_v|scaled_v|scaled_v_expr|fill_storage_slot|fill_random|qk_rowmajor_128x4|weights_times_v_128x4)\b" h src t rg -n "reinterpret_cast" h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp h/tron/tensor/v_vnni.hpp
design-r4.txt:260 states the rule. design-r4.txt:405 calls the searches an audit, and :407 gives the two patterns; neither contains v_vnni_access or k_vnni_access. design-r4.txt:251 says a row stores its pair address by value, so pointer extraction through the helper is a member read without a cast. git grep -n "vnni_access" 2880c3aa9b -- h src t returns no match, so the before-migration search says nothing about the helper and the after-migration search would list every user. Ben's sketch (github-review.json, comment 5765866077) has vnni_access::packed_data return the stored pointer. Merge note: single candidate, minor kept. Related to the sibling finding on the rg patterns' v_base/v_data noise (same command, different defect).

(2) Add the mechanism sentence: "The friend relation limits who reads the private pointer member; it does not limit who calls the helper's static function, so the rule is enforced only by review." Round 3's row said "a friend declared with the V types" [design-r3.txt:249]; the round-4 row dropped the word friend [design-wdiff.txt segment at old 3617 / new 4216], and only the K paragraph still says "internal friend helpers" [design-r4.txt:443]. Holds 3 of 3. Minor J12. Child VNNI QK identity case: its raw-plane fixture migration is not specified

Where

touches G22
Claim
The child's t_amx_numerics has a VNNI QK identity case (a test that compares qk_vnni_128x4 scores with qk_rowmajor_128x4 scores bit for bit). It holds the K plane in a raw struct, fills it with k_vnni::scatter_row through a raw pointer in a scrambled token order, and passes the raw pointer to qk_vnni_128x4. Under the design, qk_vnni_128x4 takes k_vnni_view<const bf16,64,128> by value (:418, :434), and the K owner and views have no public raw-pointer constructor (:428 applies the V rules of :259 to K). So this case cannot compile as written. The design gives fixture rules for the parent's PV case (:399-403) and for t_k_vnni_layout (:443). It names the child's identity case only for the AMX-proof log rule (:508). It never says how its fixture migrates: which owner, which fill route, whether the scrambled store order is kept, and that the row-major call wraps in->k in const_native_k_view like the parent's QK case.

Minor L02. page::scaled_v is one width-neutral body; the 8-lane view construction site is unstated

Where
5 callers table, page::scaled_v row, design-r4.txt:286; :283; 7 step 2 at :390
Earlier ids
touches G01, N16-N24
Claim
page::scaled_v has one definition for both vector widths at kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:2252-2262. It passes kv_block<geometry>(slot, kv_head).v[0] to the scaled_v_expr constructor. Both constructors take const bf16s* today (2066 and 2139). The #if TRON_CHUNK_SIZE == 16 region starts at 2281, after this body. The design retypes the two constructors differently: a packed read-only view at 16 lanes and a const_view<bf16,true,false,seq<page_size,head_size>,sseq<head_size,1>> at 8 lanes. So page::scaled_v must gain its first width branch, or kv_block must gain a width-selected accessor. The design says neither. kv_block has k_view() (2208, 2215) but no v_view(). The 8-lane const_view needs a reinterpret_cast from the bf16s array, which is a new cast site in kv_cache.hpp. The rule at :283 ("no public 8-lane V-plane accessor") can be read to forbid a kv_block::v_view() mirror of k_view(). The second rg audit (:407) will list the new cast, but :405 says to preserve only "the existing 8-lane V casts".
Evidence
Design quote and evidence
Use const_view<bf16,true,false,seq<page_size,head_size>,sseq<head_size,1>> in the 8-lane constructor, constructed internally from the block.
h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:2252-2262 (single scaled_v body, .v[0] at 2261), :2270-2279 (uniform overload forwards), :2281 #if TRON_CHUNK_SIZE == 16, :2066 and :2139 (constructors, const bf16s* data), :2208-2219 (k_view only), :1674-1720 (8-lane page::v row views with reinterpret_cast).
Correction from verification
Three corrections, then a narrowed recommendation. (1) Add the omitted fact: kv_block::v_base() exists today at [kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:2237-2240] and is unconditional (no #if between the #endif at :2202 and the closing brace at :2241), so an 8-lane plane cast already exists (reinterpret_cast<const bf16*>(v) at :2239). The design removes v_base and page::v_data [design-r4.txt:284, :393]. Their only caller chain is page::v_data [:1612] -> self_attention.hpp@2880c3aa9b:1681 (the AMX PV call). So the 8-lane const_view cast is a REPLACEMENT for a removed cast, not the first plane cast; reword "which is a new cast site in kv_cache.hpp" to "which replaces the v_base cast the design removes". (2) Fix the citation: the 8-lane page::v block is [:1674-1725], with casts at :1681, :1689, :1706, :1716, not :1674-1720. (3) Note a third route the finding does not list: the existing 8-lane page::v<geometry>(slot, kv_head, 0) const returns an ordinary row const_view whose .data member is public [:1712-1717, used at :1723], so an implementer can build the plane view from that pointer with no new reinterpret_cast; the :259 no-public-pointer rule covers only the new packed types. This strengthens the point that the design underdetermines the site (three placements are possible). Recommendation, narrowed: at design-r4.txt:286 add one sentence naming the site, for example "page::scaled_v (kv_cache.hpp:2252-2262) is one body today; give it a TRON_CHUNK_SIZE branch: at 16 lanes pass kv_block<geometry>(slot, kv_head).v.as_view(); at 8 lanes build the const_view inline from the block's V array with one reinterpret_cast, which replaces the removed v_base cast, and list it as an expected hit of the second rg audit (:407)". Or state instead that a detail::kv_block::v_view() const under #else is permitted and that :283 forbids only page-level accessors. Either sentence resolves the item.
Narrow four points and keep the recommendation. (1) Claim wording: replace "The design says neither" with "The design names the source, the block [design-r4.txt:286 'constructed internally from the block'], but not the function that builds the 8-lane view, and it never says page::scaled_v [kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:2252-2262] gains a width branch." (2) Cast claim: "needs a reinterpret_cast from the bf16s array, which is a new cast site" is one of two routes. The existing const row view v<geometry>(slot, kv_head, 0) already holds a cast pointer [kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:1709-1717], and its .data member could seed the rank-2 const_view without a new cast. State: "The 8-lane view needs either one new reinterpret_cast of the bf16s array, mirroring k_view() const [:2215-2219], or a reuse of the existing v<geometry>(slot, kv_head, 0).data pointer. The design should choose, and the audit sentence at :405 should name the result." (3) The :283 point: add its origin. The rule came from round-2 N23, whose recommendation was page-level ("The 8-lane build adds no plane accessor"). Recommend the design write "no public page-level 8-lane V-plane accessor" or state that a detail::kv_block accessor is allowed. (4) Add one fact that limits the finding: the definition of done restricts only "Packed physical indexing" to tensor operations and kernels [design-r4.txt:533], so a new 8-lane row-major cast in cache code breaks no stated rule; the gap is an omission in :286 and :405, not a contradiction. Also add: this text is unchanged since round 3 [design-r3.txt:273]; round-2 N24 was graded resolved on it [r3-findings.md:1163]; relates_to should read "G01, N23, N24" rather than "N16-N24". Recommendation text stays as written; both offered routes are consistent with the design (16 lanes: kv_block<geometry>(slot, kv_head).v.as_view(), matching :285's .v.as_view() form; 8 lanes: the inline const_view or a kv_block::v_view() const under #else).
Narrow the claim and lower the severity to note. Corrected claim: "page::scaled_v is one width-neutral body [kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:2252-2262]. The design types the two scaled_v_expr constructors differently [design-r4.txt:286] but says only that the 8-lane const_view is 'constructed internally from the block'. It does not name page::scaled_v as the body that gains a TRON_CHUNK_SIZE branch, nor say that the 8-lane branch adds one reinterpret_cast over the raw array, in the pattern of kv_block::k_view() [:2214-2219]. The wording is unchanged from round 3 [design-r3.txt:273], where it resolved N24. Both builds fail to compile until the implementer writes both branches, so the omission cannot produce a wrong build." Drop the sub-claim that :405 covers only "the existing 8-lane V casts": :405 also names "the permitted weighted-sum kernel" as a legitimate home for V-plane address extraction and says the search is not a zero-match test. The second audit (:407) should expect that one new cast." Keep the alternative (a detail-level kv_block::v_view() const under #else) as equally acceptable and state that :283 forbids page-level accessors only. Relates to N23, N24 (round 2) and G01 (round 3), not to N16-N22.
Recommendation
At :286 add: 'page::scaled_v (kv_cache.hpp:2252-2262) is one body for both widths and passes kv_block<geometry>(slot, kv_head).v[0] to both constructors. Give it a TRON_CHUNK_SIZE branch: at 16 lanes pass the block owner's v.as_view(); under #else construct the const_view named in this row from the block, either with one reinterpret_cast of the bf16s array in the pattern of kv_block::k_view() const (this cast replaces the v_base cast the design removes) or from the existing 8-lane row view's public .data pointer. Lead's reading
Holds 3 of 3 (intent at note). page::scaled_v is one body outside every width block and today passes .v[0] to both constructors; the design types them differently and says only constructed internally from the block. Both builds fail to compile until the branch is written, so the risk is a stall, not wrong bytes. Corrections: the 8-lane const_view cast replaces the v_base cast the design removes (kv_cache.hpp:2237-2240), the existing 8-lane row view's public .data pointer is a second route, and the :283 rule came from round-2 N23 and forbids page-level accessors only.

Note J08. load_row for float destinations duplicates row.chunk() plus view::set

Where
5. Interface design, extraction paragraph and row-write rule (design-r4.txt:257, :262, :268); 8. instruction-comparison bullet (:523)
Earlier ids
touches G13 F38
Claim
The design puts the even/odd extraction into v_vnni_row::chunk() (:257, :262) and separately moves today's get_v loops into load_row (:268). For a float destination these are the same instructions written twice. Today's get_v(float*) does, per 16 dimensions, one aligned 64-byte load, a shift-left-16 (even token) or an and-with-0xffff0000 (odd token), and one _mm512_store_ps. The existing rank-1 view::set(expr<X,d0> const&) already loops chunky<float,true>::set(data, i, x.chunk(i)), and chunky<float,true>::set is tron_mm_store_ps. So dst.set(src[token]) with the proposed row expression is the float unpack; a hand-written float load_row is a second copy of the same mechanism. Only the bf16 destination needs its own loop, because chunky<bf16,true>::set converts through fp32s_to_bf16s instead of the direct _mm512_cvtepi32_epi16 narrowing that :262 and :523 preserve. The float unpack has no production caller: full.hpp's callback and t_llama_unit.cpp:819, :1575, :2038 unpack to bf16 or float per site; the float callers are tests only.
Evidence
Design quote and evidence
load_row moves today's get_v loops. Wrap existing caller-owned scratch in the ordinary contiguous destination view.
design-r4.txt:257 ('Read chunk i at pair_base + 2 * chunk_size * i and extract the even or odd half'), :262 ('Copy the extraction pattern from the existing float get_v loop into v_vnni_row::chunk(): shift left by 16 bits for even tokens and mask with 0xffff0000 for odd tokens'), :268, :523. h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:2412-2432 get_v(float*): _mm512_load_si512(v_ptr + i * 2), _mm512_slli_epi32(both, 16) / _mm512_and_si512(both, hi_16_of_32), _mm512_store_ps(vout_ptr + i, ...) at :2418-2421 and :2427-2430; get_v(bf16*) narrowing _mm256_store_si256 after _mm512_cvtepi32_epi16 at :2452-2455 and :2460-2463. h/tron/tensor/view.hpp@2880c3aa9b:187-191 view& set(expr<X, d0> const& x) noexcept { if constexpr (s0 == 1 && writeable_in_chunks<T, aligned>) for (size_t i = 0; i < d0 / chunk_size; ++i) chunky<T, aligned>::set(data, i, x.chunk(i));. h/tron/kernels/chunky.hpp@2880c3aa9b:30-35 chunky<float, true>::set = tron_mm_store_ps(data + i * chunk_size, value); :50-58 chunky<bf16, true>::set = tron_mm_store_bf16(..., fp32s_to_bf16s(value)). Callers: h/tron/scheduler/full.hpp@2880c3aa9b:2652-2657 (bf16 dest); t/t_llama_unit.cpp get_v at :819, :1575, :2038, :2539, :2761, :2824 (facts-r4.md :282 and :523 entries). Merge note: single candidate, minor kept.
Correction from verification
Keep the claim and severity. Apply these narrowings. (1) Cite the sentence "load_row moves today's get_v loops. Wrap existing caller-owned scratch in the ordinary contiguous destination view." as design-r4.txt:272, not :268; :268 is the parameter-family code line. (2) Fix ranges: get_v(float*) body is kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:2412-2433; view::set is view.hpp@2880c3aa9b:188-191 (template line :186); chunky<float, true> spans chunky.hpp@2880c3aa9b:29-37 with set at :34-36. (3) Hedge the added :523 sentence: "The float unpack is expected to show the baseline's load, shift-or-mask and store_ps sequence once the compiler hoists the invariant parity test of row.chunk() out of the D/16-chunk loop (the row stores parity by value, design-r4.txt:251). A per-chunk branch or blend absent from the baseline get_v(float*) is the signature of a missed hoist and is a reason to keep the two-loop shape." Either choice resolves J08; specifying two copies does not.

Note J14. Native-branch K copy is called an array copy; it is a row-range memcpy

Where
7. Child storage and access, Copy and score readers row (design-r4.txt:434)
Earlier ids
touches G44
Claim
The row says to use defaulted owner assignment for the packed whole-page copy and then to 'Retain the existing array copy in the native branch'. The native branch (the row-major K layout branch of copy_storage_slot) does not copy an array. It computes k_size = head_size * sizeof(bf16), takes k_at(src_begin).data and k_at(dst_begin).data, and runs memcpy(dst, src, count * k_size). It copies count token rows from the given offsets, so a partial token range copies only that range. Placed right after the owner-assignment sentence, 'array copy' reads as a whole-array copy. The K member in that branch is a C array (bf16s k[page_size][head_size / chunk_size]) and cannot be assigned, so a wrong reading fails to compile rather than silently copying the wrong tokens. The word 'existing' anchors the meaning. The wording is inexact but low risk.
Evidence
Design quote and evidence
Use defaulted owner assignment, as for V. Retain the existing array copy in the native branch. Add no K plane-copy helper.
h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp@30c4ac82cb:2117-2127 (native branch): constexpr size_t k_size = geometry.head_size * sizeof(bf16); ... .k_at(src_begin).data ... .k_at(dst_begin).data; memcpy(dst, src, count * k_size);. The packed branch's whole-page copy is memcpy(dst_block.k, src_block.k, sizeof(dst_block.k)) at :2107, and its partial-range loop is gather_row/scatter_row at :2109-2115. Main has the same native memcpy at h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:1933-1941. The K array is declared alignas(kv_block_alignment) bf16s k[page_size][head_size / chunk_size]; at kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:2194. design-r4.txt:434, :416, :428, :470, :574, :597. Merge note: two candidates (one minor, one note); minor kept because both ask for a wording change and the sentence is new in round 4.
Correction from verification
Narrow three details. (1) The design is not silent on the mechanism. Section 6 already says "Preserve native-K and 8-lane V row-range byte copies" [design-r4.txt:376]. The imprecision is local to the section-7 row [:434]. Reword the claim to: "Section 6 [:376] names the native K copy correctly as a row-range byte copy; the section-7 row [:434] calls the same code an 'array copy', which does not match." (2) The "fails to compile" sentence covers only one wrong reading. dst_block.k = src_block.k on the C array is ill-formed, so that reading fails to compile. A whole-array memcpy(dst_block.k, src_block.k, sizeof(dst_block.k)) rewrite would compile and would copy 64 rows for a partial range. "Retain the existing" instructs no rewrite, so the risk stays low. Reword to: "One wrong reading (owner assignment of the C array) fails to compile; a whole-array memcpy rewrite would compile, but 'Retain the existing' forbids a rewrite." (3) The design's own term "array branch" [:428, "Constrain native k_view() and k_at() to the array branch"] gives "array copy" a consistent internal reading (the copy in the array branch). Say so; it lowers the risk further. Recommended replacement sentence for :434: "Retain the native branch's existing row-range memcpy of count rows from k_at(src_begin).data to k_at(dst_begin).data (child kv_cache.hpp@30c4ac82cb:2117-2127; main 2880c3aa9b:1933-1941), as section 6 [:376] already requires."
If the lead keeps this as a note, narrow it to an optional wording tweak, not an ambiguity: "The section-7 row [design-r4.txt:434] says 'Retain the existing array copy in the native branch'. Section 6 already names this copy precisely as a native-K row-range byte copy [design-r4.txt:376], and 'array' is the design's name for the native storage branch [design-r4.txt:426, :428]. Optional: reword the row to 'Retain the native branch's existing row-range memcpy of count rows from k_at(src_begin) to k_at(dst_begin) [kv_cache.hpp@30c4ac82cb:2117-2127]' so the row is self-contained." Drop the claim that the phrase "reads as a whole-array copy"; "retain the existing" forbids writing any new copy, and a whole-array assignment of the C array does not compile. Severity note, not minor. Relates to G44 (resolved by the round-4 text).
Narrow the claim and lower the severity to note. Replace the sentence "Placed right after the owner-assignment sentence, 'array copy' reads as a whole-array copy" with: "The design names the native branch 'the array branch' at design-r4.txt:428, so 'array copy in the native branch' can be read as 'the copy in the array branch'. The phrase is compressed rather than misleading." Replace "a wrong reading fails to compile rather than silently copying the wrong tokens" with: "The owner-assignment misreading (dst_block.k = src_block.k on the C array at kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:2194) does not compile. A whole-array memcpy misreading would compile, but 'Retain the existing' asks for no new code." Keep the recommendation as an optional wording change: "Retain the native branch's existing row-range memcpy of count rows from k_at(src_begin) to k_at(dst_begin) (child kv_cache.hpp@30c4ac82cb:2117-2127, identical to main kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:1933-1941)." State that G44 is resolved and J14 is a residual wording note on the new round-4 sentence [design-wdiff.txt:392-396].
Recommendation
Optional: replace 'Retain the existing array copy in the native branch.' (:434) with 'Retain the native branch's existing row-range memcpy of count rows from k_at(src_begin) to k_at(dst_begin) (child kv_cache.hpp:2117-2127, identical to main kv_cache.hpp:1933-1941).'
Lead's reading
Holds 2 of 3; lowered to note by the lead. Section 6 (:376) already names the native K copy as a row-range byte copy, and array branch is the design's own name for the native storage branch (:428), so the section-7 phrase is compressed rather than misleading. Optional rewording only.

behavior-arena, disposition-checker; 2 of 3 verifiers could not refute it (votes and corrections)

Note J16. 'data member' written where the reviewer's constraint is 'no data()'

Where
2. Proposed issue #4525 text, sixth Scope bullet (design-r4.txt:127); 5. View copy and conversion row (:259)
Earlier ids
touches F20
Claim
The sixth Scope bullet and the body say 'data member' where the reviewer's constraint is 'no data()'. In C++, 'data member' means a member variable; a data() accessor is a member function that returns a raw pointer. The string 'data()' appears nowhere in design-r4.txt. An implementer who keeps the array private has satisfied the text as written and could still add a public data() accessor. This wording is not new (design-r3.txt:118 and :248 had it) and the round-3 fairness lens offered 'data() accessor' as optional polish.
Evidence
Design quote and evidence
Packed owners and views expose no public raw-pointer constructor, data member, or pointer conversion.
design-r4.txt:127 and :259 ('data member'); grep 'data()' over design-r4.txt: 0 hits. github-review.json comments[3].body (comment 5765866077): 'There is no data(), pointer conversion, or operator[] that suggests a contiguous row.'; the sketch keeps data_ private and has no data(). design-r3.txt:118 and :248 (same wording in round 3). Section 9 :585 'Add the no-cast arena constraint.' Merge note: single candidate, note kept.
Correction from verification
Narrow the recommendation from "replace" to "add". "No public data member" is not an error: it requires the stored pointer or array itself to be private, as the sketch does (private: T* data_; and private: std::array<bf16, ...> data_; [comment 5765866077]). Replacing it with "data() accessor" would drop that constraint. Suggested text at design-r4.txt:127: "Packed owners and views expose no public raw-pointer constructor, data member, data() accessor, or pointer conversion." At :259: "Owners, views, and rows expose no public raw-pointer constructor, data member, data() accessor, or pointer conversion." Optional: in the typed-boundary test row [:465] add "Reject a data() member on packed matrices and rows" beside "Reject raw-pointer construction". Citation detail: the JSON path is comments.comments[3].body, not comments[3].body. Relation to F20: this does not reopen F20; its round-3 residual asked only for the no-cast wording, which :127 now carries.
Three narrowings. (1) Lower the implementer-risk claim: the design already blocks the substance through Q3's "no public pointer" for rows [design-r4.txt:104], the detail::v_vnni_access-only address route [design-r4.txt:260], and the audit rule that V-plane address extraction belongs only to typed operations, the weighted-sum kernel, or the whole-block fill exception [design-r4.txt:405]. The defect is chiefly that the prepared issue text at :127 and the contract row at :259 misrender the reviewer's "no data()" constraint. (2) "data member" is not wrong in this codebase; it is incomplete. The ordinary tron view exposes its pointer as a public data member named data (T* data; at h/tron/tensor/view.hpp@2880c3aa9b:40 and :121; the native-QK caller reads k0.data at self_attention.hpp@2880c3aa9b:1653-1655, cited by design-r4.txt:280). The round-2 text wrote ".data member" with a dot [r2-findings.md:244, :335], meaning "no public field like view::data". So the fix should keep both exclusions, for example: "expose no public raw-pointer constructor, no public data member or data() accessor, and no pointer conversion" at :127 and :259. Writing "data() accessor" in place of "data member" would drop the field exclusion. (3) Add G05 to relates_to: the round-3 G05 fairness correction asked for exactly this wording change [r3-findings.md:1118], and the G05 recommendation codex followed carried "data member" itself [r3-findings.md:105], which explains why the slip survived.
Narrow the claim from "'data member' written where the constraint is 'no data()'" to: "The sentence names the public data field but not a public data() accessor; on a strict C++ reading an accessor is not excluded." Add the fact that "data member" is tron-idiomatic and should be KEPT: tron's own view exposes a public T* data; field (h/tron/tensor/view.hpp@2880c3aa9b:40; used as k0.data at self_attention.hpp@2880c3aa9b:1655), so the design's phrase forbids the most likely copy-paste leak. Change the recommendation from replacing to extending: at design-r4.txt:127 and :259 write "no public raw-pointer constructor, data member, data() accessor, or pointer conversion" (add, do not substitute, so the public-field exclusion stays). Note in the evidence that design-r4.txt:104 ("no public pointer") and :260 (detail::v_vnni_access as the storage route with a closed user list) already carry the intent, which is why the severity is note. Cite batches/B06.md:23 (fairness-lens optional polish) and B06.md:21 (F20 STILL NEEDED after round 3 asked nothing about data()) to show this was never a required change.
Recommendation
Write 'no public raw-pointer constructor, data member, data() accessor, or pointer conversion' at :127 and :259 (add the accessor, keep the data-member exclusion). Optional: in the typed-boundary row (:465) add 'Reject a data() member on packed matrices and rows' beside 'Reject raw-pointer construction'.
Lead's reading
Holds 3 of 3 at note. Correction: add data() accessor, do not substitute it. No public data member is right for tron, whose ordinary view exposes a public data field (view.hpp:40), and the sketch keeps data_ private; the sentence lacks only the accessor.

Note J21. Class key for the new packed-type definitions is not stated (clang -Wmismatched-tags)

Where
5. Interface design, declarations (design-r4.txt:243-245); 7. child K forward declarations (:418)
Earlier ids
touches G08
Claim
kv_cache_fwd.hpp declares v_vnni_tensor, v_vnni_view and v_vnni_row with the class key, and the child's k_vnni_* declarations are to be parallel. The design says to use the struct key for the existing bf16, view and const_view, but does not say which key the new definitions in v_vnni.hpp and k_vnni.hpp use. Every existing tensor type in the repository is a struct (view, const_view, views, tensor, kv_block, scaled_v_expr), so an implementer following the repository's style would write struct definitions. The pinned compiler is clang++-19 with -Wall, and clang's -Wmismatched-tags (enabled by -Wall) warns when a declaration and its definition use different keys. The code still compiles (no -Werror found), so this is a warning, not a build break.
Evidence
Design quote and evidence

design-r4.txt:243-245 (declarations and the struct-key sentence), :418 ('Add unconstrained k_vnni_tensor, k_vnni_view, and k_vnni_row forward declarations to h/tron/tensor/kv_cache_fwd.hpp, parallel to the V declarations'). Existing keys: h/tron/tensor/views.hpp@2880c3aa9b:22-34 (struct view, struct const_view), h/tron/tensor/view.hpp@2880c3aa9b:25,116,263,276,361 (struct), h/tron/tensor/tensor.hpp@2880c3aa9b:25,30 (struct tensor), h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:2190 (struct kv_block), :1989 (struct scaled_v_expr). Compiler and flags: CMakePresets.json@2880c3aa9b:19,56 (CMAKE_CXX_COMPILER clang++-19), CMakeLists.txt@2880c3aa9b:609 (-Wall -Wpedantic -Wextra for GCC or Clang); git grep for -Werror in CMakeLists.txt, src/tron/CMakeLists.txt, t/CMakeLists.txt and cmake/ at 2880c3aa9b finds none. Local check: g++ -std=c++20 -Wall -fsyntax-only accepts the design's five forward declarations and both aliases as written (scratchpad agent-notes/alias_test.cpp); GCC has no mismatched-tag warning, clang does. Note J22. Declarations header forward-declares owner and row templates that no named includer uses

Where
5. Interface design, declarations (design-r4.txt:243-245) and build boundaries (:299); 7. child header split (:418)
Earlier ids
touches G08 G16
Claim
kv_cache_fwd.hpp forward-declares v_vnni_tensor and v_vnni_row (:243) and, in the child, k_vnni_tensor and k_vnni_row (:418). The only includer the design names is amx_attn_iface.hpp (:299). Its typed declarations name only const_native_k_view and v_vnni_view (:290) and, in the child, k_vnni_view (:418). Note J23. The rewritten helper row dropped the word 'friend'

Where

Earlier ids
touches G01
Claim
The round-4 helper row no longer says that detail::v_vnni_access is a friend of the V types. The only C++ mechanism by which the helper can reach a pointer that the views do not expose is left implicit. The child paragraph still says 'avoid the internal friend helpers' (:443), so a reader can recover the intent.

Claim

The contract says only 'Allow writable-to-read-only conversion only' (:259) and 'Accept writable-to-read-only matrix conversion only' (:465); neither says the converting constructor is implicit. touches G01

Note J27. Model save: the exact packed-branch source-row expression is not given

Where
7. step 3 Model save (design-r4.txt:391); 5. One cache row-write rule and callers table (:270, :281)
Earlier ids
touches G41 N15
Claim
Step 3 says to wrap aligned source rows in the packed branch. The current 16-lane branch passes a raw pointer into the whole kv_width buffer row. The wrapper must first slice one head's row and then construct the read-only view explicitly, because deduction of the const_view parameter does not see a writable view. The design gives neither the slice nor the expected view type, although the 8-lane branch three lines below already shows the slice. The dma value of the wrapped view depends on the sibling finding about the model buffer's DMA flag, so the recommendation here leaves that flag as 'the buffer's own'.
Evidence
Design quote and evidence
Wrap aligned source rows in the packed branch.
h/tron/models/model.hpp@2880c3aa9b:2833-2834 pg.template set_v<geometry.kv>(slot, kv_head, j, &vs[ix][kv_head * geometry.kv.head_size]);; :2836-2838 pg.template v<geometry.kv>(slot, kv_head, j).set(slice<geometry.kv.head_size>()(vs[ix].as_view(), kv_head * geometry.kv.head_size));. h/tron/tensor/slice.hpp@2880c3aa9b:44-78: the view overload returns view<T, aligned, dma, seq<d0, ...>, sseq<s0, ...>> with the input's flags and asserts last_offset * sizeof(T) % 32 == 0 when aligned. design-r4.txt:270 states that deduction does not see writable views. Merge note: single candidate, note kept; the literal 'false' dma flag of the original recommendation was replaced by 'the buffer's own flag' pending the sibling finding.
Correction from verification
Narrow two details and widen one. (1) The claim "gives neither the slice nor the expected view type" overstates: the type FAMILY const_view<Source, true, Dma, seq<D>, sseq<1>> is given [design-r4.txt:266] and :281 points at it. Reword to: "The design gives the parameter family but not the concrete model-site instantiation (Source, Dma, D) or the slice expression that produces it." (2) The recommendation's const_view<bf16, true, Dma, ...> is too specific. Source is executor-dependent: bf16 for hardware::btensor = dmatensor<bf16> (tp1/tp2/tp4 and host_bf16) [btensor.hpp@2880c3aa9b:21; common.hpp@2880c3aa9b:94-101], float for the host executor's tensor [common.hpp:100; tensor.hpp@2880c3aa9b:71], which t_llama_unit uses [t/t_llama_unit.cpp@2880c3aa9b:1252, 1298, 1323, 1852, 1986], and fp16 for htensor = dmatensor<fp16> [h/tron/hardware/htensor.hpp@2880c3aa9b:8]. Write const_view<Source, true, Dma, seq<geometry.kv.head_size>, sseq<1>> where Source and Dma are the element type and dma flag of decltype(vs[ix].as_view()). Dma is true for the production dmatensor buffers [dmatensor.hpp@2880c3aa9b:223] and false only for the host executor's tensor [tensor.hpp:71]. (3) "The wrapper must construct the read-only view explicitly" is one route, not the only one: slice<geometry.kv.head_size>()(std::as_const(vs[ix]).as_view(), kv_head * geometry.kv.head_size) yields a const_view directly, because the const as_view() overloads return const_view [dmatensor.hpp@2880c3aa9b:227-229; tensor.hpp:75] and slice's const_view overload returns a const_view [slice.hpp@2880c3aa9b:80-116, return at :113-115]. Offer either form. (4) The alignment sentence holds for bf16 and fp16 (byte offset = kv_head * head_size * 2, a multiple of 64); for a float buffer it is a multiple of 128. Say "at least a multiple of 64 bytes". (5) Keep the note that Dma at :270 ("The model source already has a view with dma = false") is wrong for production buffers; that belongs to the sibling finding, and J27's "the buffer's own flag" wording is right as it stands. Suggested step-3 sentence: "In save_v_impl replace the pointer with slice<geometry.kv.head_size>()(std::as_const(vs[ix]).as_view(), kv_head * geometry.kv.head_size), or construct const_view<Source, true, Dma, seq<geometry.kv.head_size>, sseq<1>> from the 8-lane branch's slice at model.hpp@2880c3aa9b:2836-2838, with Source and Dma taken from the buffer's own as_view() type. The buffer view is aligned = true and the offset is a multiple of 64 bytes because set_v requires head_size % 32 == 0."
Narrow the claim to: "Step 3 [design-r4.txt:391] and the set_v row [:281] tell the implementer to wrap the aligned source row but do not write the wrapping expression for the model's 16-lane branch, which today passes a raw pointer into the whole kv_width row [model.hpp@2880c3aa9b:2833-2834]. The type family [:266], the Source/aligned/Dma rules and the explicit read-only construction rule [:270] are already stated, so only the head-row slice is missing. The 8-lane branch shows it [model.hpp@2880c3aa9b:2836-2838]." Drop "nor the expected view type". Fix the recommendation in two places. (1) Do not hard-code bf16. The buffer scalar depends on the executor: tp1/tp2/tp4 and host_bf16 use hardware::btensor = dmatensor<bf16> [h/tron/models/common.hpp@2880c3aa9b:94-99, :101; h/tron/hardware/btensor.hpp:21], host_fp16 uses htensor = dmatensor<fp16> [common.hpp:102; h/tron/hardware/htensor.hpp:8], and host uses tensor, whose rows are float [common.hpp:100; h/tron/tensor/tensor.hpp:71]. That is why set_v has bf16, fp16 and float overloads [kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:1622-1631] and why the design deduces Source [design-r4.txt:266, :270]. Suggested step-3 sentence: "In save_v_impl build the source as slice<geometry.kv.head_size>()(vs[ix].as_view(), kv_head * geometry.kv.head_size), the expression the 8-lane branch already uses [model.hpp@2880c3aa9b:2836-2838], and convert it to the read-only const_view of the same scalar, alignment and Dma before calling page::set_v; slice keeps the buffer view's flags [slice.hpp@2880c3aa9b:75-77]. The offset kv_head * head_size * 2 bytes is a multiple of 64 because set_v requires head_size % 32 == 0." (2) Replace "Confirm the buffer view's aligned flag is true" with a fact: both buffer families return aligned = true views [dmatensor.hpp@2880c3aa9b:223-228; tensor.hpp@2880c3aa9b:71-75]. Also note for the sibling finding, not for J27: the buffer view's Dma flag is true for dmatensor (all production executors) and false only for the host executor's tensor [dmatensor.hpp:223, :227; tensor.hpp:71, :75], so the design's sentence "The model source already has a view with dma = false" [design-r4.txt:270] is wrong for production builds; slice simply keeps whichever flag the buffer carries.
Narrow the claim to: "Step 3 [design-r4.txt:391] and the callers table [:281] name the target parameter type [:268] but never write the construction expression for the 16-lane model branch. The current branch passes a raw pointer into the whole kv_width row [model.hpp@2880c3aa9b:2833-2834]; the 8-lane branch three lines below shows the slice [model.hpp:2836-2838]. Deduction against const_view<Source, true, Dma, seq<D>, sseq<1>> does not see a writable view, so the implementer must either slice a const view or convert the sliced writable view." Drop "neither ... the expected view type" (the generic type is at :268). Replace "must ... construct the read-only view explicitly" with "must obtain a const_view, either from std::as_const(vs[ix]).as_view() [dmatensor.hpp@2880c3aa9b:226-229; tensor.hpp@2880c3aa9b:73-75] through the const_view slice overload [slice.hpp@2880c3aa9b:80-115], or by naming const_view<...> around the sliced writable view [view.hpp@2880c3aa9b:385]". Replace the recommendation with: "In step 3 write: 'Build the packed-branch source as slice<geometry.kv.head_size>()(std::as_const(vs[ix]).as_view(), kv_head * geometry.kv.head_size), the same slice the 8-lane branch uses [model.hpp@2880c3aa9b:2836-2838], taken from the const as_view() so that the result is already a const_view<Scalar, true, Dma, seq<geometry.kv.head_size>, sseq<1>>. Both as_view() forms return aligned = true [dmatensor.hpp:223-229; tensor.hpp:71-75]. The slice assertion last_offset * sizeof(T) % 32 == 0 [slice.hpp:57-63] holds because set_v requires head_size % 32 == 0 [kv_cache.hpp:1623].'" Do not write bf16 or * 2 as fixed values. Keep severity note, kind ambiguity, relates_to G41 N15.

Note J28. Fixture rule forbids the smaller byte-copy route that round-3 G22 left open

Where
7. AMX numerics fixture migration (design-r4.txt:401; mirrored for the child at :443)
Earlier ids
touches G22
Claim
Round-3 G22's lead note and intent verifier asked the design to state whether a test may copy hand-packed bytes into the trivially copyable owner, and said to prefer the append route. The round-4 text turns that into a prohibition for 'these fixtures'. The byte copy is the smallest change to the PV case: one memcpy of 16,384 bytes into a v_vnni_tensor<64,128> and owner.as_view() at the kernel call, with the existing hand-packed std::vector, bf16_rne inputs and reference untouched. It also keeps the case's stated purpose, kernel numerics against an independent input, separate from the write path. The chosen route makes the PV numerics case depend on append_v_row, which the t_llama_unit 'V append and padding' row already covers, and adds 64 aligned bf16_rne source rows, 64 append calls, a byte copy-out and a compare. The design states its reason and concedes the copy is legal C++, so this is a smallest-change judgment, not an error.
Evidence
Design quote and evidence
Do not copy hand-packed input bytes into the owner in these fixtures. This fixture rule verifies the public typed write path. It is not a claim that byte copying into a trivially copyable owner is illegal C++.
design-r4.txt:397-403, :443, :462 ('V append and padding: t_llama_unit, 16-lane build'). t/t_amx_numerics.cpp@2880c3aa9b:6 (header comment: 'for PV, the weight rounding mode - AVX scaled_v truncates'), :199 (std::vector<float> v), :203-210 (hand-packed std::vector<tron::bf16> v_page(PAGE_TOKENS_64 * HEAD_SIZE_128) filled with the pair formula via bf16_rne), :225-226 (weights_times_v_128x4(v_page.data(), &exp_rows[0][0], PAGE_TOKENS_64, o_amx)). Owner size 64 x 128 x 2 bytes = 16,384 bytes (design-r4.txt:398; facts-r4.md). r3-findings.md G22 LEAD NOTE: 'a memcpy of hand-packed bytes into the trivially copyable owner is legal C++, so the design should state whether tests may do it rather than the finding forbidding it'; intent-lens correction: 'State whether a memcpy of hand-packed bytes into the trivially copyable owner is allowed in tests. Prefer the append_v_row route'. Merge note: single candidate, note kept.

Note J31. K expression consumer can run in required CI with a standalone owner; the design leaves it open

Where
7. Child storage and access (design-r4.txt:439, :441); 8. CI ownership (:494)
Claim
The design leaves required CI execution of the new K expression check as an open child obligation. A resolution follows from the design's own rules and it is not stated. The K class definitions and their 16-lane operation bodies do not depend on the TRON_K_VNNI option; only layout_on does. So a K expression check that builds a standalone k_vnni_tensor owner, fills it with typed store_row calls and constructs a host tensor from owner.as_view() compiles and runs in the required 16-lane Test host build, where TRON_K_VNNI is off. Only checks that read a page through page::k_packed need the option. Saying this lets the child place the expression check where it executes in required CI instead of deferring the question.

Note J32. 'Copy paths' listed as set_k_row/get_k_row const_view construction sites; the child's copy code calls neither

Where
