Earlier ids
touches G46
Claim
The row tells the implementer to construct read-only source views explicitly 'in model save paths, copy paths, and affected test callers'. The child's copy code never calls set_k_row or get_k_row. copy_storage_slot copies a whole page with memcpy and moves partial ranges with k_vnni::gather_row and k_vnni::scatter_row directly. The design routes those copies through owner assignment and typed token copies (:434), which take matrix views, not row views. In that function the source block is auto const&, so k.as_view() const & already yields the read-only view and no explicit construction is needed. Note J35. make test-host refuses on a build host without avx512f

Where
8. Commands and execution hosts (design-r4.txt:513-514); Build configurations (:490)
Claim
The design runs the local gate 'make build-test, and make test-host' on the build host (:513-514) and, when that host lacks AVX-512, switches to the cross-avx512 preset (:490). The Makefile decides the preset from /proc/cpuinfo. Without avx512f it selects cross-avx512 and sets CANNOT_RUN_SLICE_TESTS := 1 (unless REMOTE_ENABLED is set). With that variable set, make test-host does not run Slice; it prints 'Error: Cannot run test-host: cross-compiled for AVX-512. Run tests on a machine with AVX-512.' and exits 1. The design does not say where the host suite runs in that case. Insufficient data: this review did not check the CPU flags of sw-dev-01 (codex's build host), so the condition may or may not apply there.
Evidence
Design quote and evidence
Run targeted suites, then make format, make lint, make lint-notes, make build-test, and make test-host in the normal 16-lane AMX-off configuration.
GNUmakefile@2880c3aa9b:227-231 (HAS_AVX512 from grep -q avx512f /proc/cpuinfo; CMAKE_PRESET := native or cross-avx512), :243-247 (CANNOT_RUN_SLICE_TESTS := 1 when HAS_AVX512 = no and REMOTE_ENABLED is empty), :901-903 (test-host test-fpga test-fast ...: @echo "Error: Cannot run $@: cross-compiled for AVX-512. Run tests on a machine with AVX-512." >&2; exit 1), :905-906 (the normal recipe bin/slice run --filter=host --exclude-tag=slow). design-r4.txt:490, :513-514. Merge note: single candidate, note kept.

Note J39. Section 9 over-states what the AMX-off compile of the fake kernels checks

Where
9. Disposition, round-3 table, row G14 G47 (design-r4.txt:559)
Earlier ids
touches G14 G47
Claim
The section-9 G14/G47 row gives a benefit that does not exist: 'This preserves declaration checking by unconditional fake kernels in AMX-off builds.' With TRON_AMX_DISPATCH off nothing calls the kernels (all amx_attn_h128g4 uses in self_attention.hpp sit inside #ifdef TRON_AMX_DISPATCH blocks) and amx_attn.cpp is not compiled. A fake in t_amx_dispatch_dtype.cpp whose parameter list drifts from the header declaration is then an unrelated overload and compiles without error. The drift surfaces only in an AMX-on link, where the real amx_attn.o is pulled in. The round-3 verifiers (source and design-text lenses) said the same: the AMX-off compile 'checks nothing extra'. The AMX-off compile checks only that the fake bodies compile against complete class types. The body at :296 gives a different, valid reason (avoid an extra test refactor) and is not affected.
Evidence
Design quote and evidence
Keep class definitions complete in both vector widths. This preserves declaration checking by unconditional fake kernels in AMX-off builds.
h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp@2880c3aa9b:259-267, :281-290, :1429-1464, :1531-1538, :1571-1701, :1748-1771 (every amx_attn_h128g4 use inside #ifdef TRON_AMX_DISPATCH). src/tron/CMakeLists.txt@2880c3aa9b:219-224 (amx_attn.cpp compiled only under the option). t/t_amx_dispatch_dtype.cpp@2880c3aa9b:166-190 (fake definitions outside any #ifdef). design-r4.txt:296, :389, :441, :480, :481, :559. r3-findings.md G14 verifier corrections (AMX-off compile 'checks nothing extra'). Merge note: single candidate, note kept.
Correction from verification
Three narrowings; none changes the verdict. 1. Narrow "The AMX-off compile checks only class completeness." It also catches one drift class: a same-parameter definition whose return type or exception specification differs from the declaration is a compile error in every build (probe: g++ reports "ambiguating new declaration" for int qk_rowmajor_128x4(const bf16*, const bf16*, float*, size_t) against the void declaration). Only a parameter-list drift compiles silently. Suggested wording for design-r4.txt:559: "The AMX-off compile of the fakes checks class completeness and same-parameter return-type agreement only. The fakes stay where they are to avoid an extra test refactor. A parameter-list drift is caught only in the AMX-on configuration." 2. Say how the drift surfaces in the AMX-on link, so the recommendation is precise. With AMX on, the typed call in self_attention.hpp references a symbol the drifted fake no longer defines. The linker then pulls amx_attn.o out of the static libtron.a. That object defines all six functions in one translation unit [src/tron/kernels/amx_attn.cpp@2880c3aa9b:76, :88, :98, :102, :122, :188, inside namespace tron::amx_attn_h128g4 at :28]. Its other five definitions collide with the remaining fakes, so the link fails with multiple-definition errors. "Caught only by the AMX-on link" is right; the mechanism is a multiple-definition error, not an undefined reference. 3. Fairness: the design's sentence echoes the round-3 lead note on G14, "Cost of the move: every AMX-off configuration, including the required Nix lane, stops compiling any kernel definition against the typed declarations" [r3-findings.md, G14 LEAD NOTE]. The three round-3 verifier corrections narrowed that cost to "not a functional loss" (source lens item 3), "checks nothing extra" (design-text lens item c) and "That lost check is small" (intent lens item b). Codex adopted the lead's framing rather than the corrections. State this so the finding reads as a wording fix, not a codex error. Evidence nit: the finding lists self_attention.hpp :281-290 and :1531-1538 among the amx_attn_h128g4 uses. Both blocks are guarded by #ifdef TRON_AMX_DISPATCH, but neither names amx_attn_h128g4 (git grep at 2880c3aa9b). Cite them as guarded AMX blocks, not as kernel uses. The conclusion that every use is guarded is unchanged.
Three narrowings, none changing the verdict. (1) Fairness: the phrase echoes the round-3 lead note on G14 ("every AMX-off configuration ... stops compiling any kernel definition against the typed declarations") [r3-findings.md, G14 LEAD NOTE]; the round-3 design-text verifier narrowed it to "checks nothing extra" and the source lens to "only observable with AMX on", while the intent lens called the lost check "small" but asked the design to weigh it. Say that the wording originated in the review's own lead note and that the three round-3 lenses did not all say the same. (2) Keep one real benefit precise: at 8 lanes with AMX off, the fakes are the only instantiation of the packed class templates, because kv_cache.hpp uses the packed V owner only in 16-lane blocks [design-r4.txt:390] and the 8-lane row-major cache is retained [:297]; a by-value parameter in a definition forces completeness, so the fakes do check that the packed classes compile at 8 lanes. At 16 lanes AMX off, kv_cache.hpp already instantiates them, so nothing extra is checked. Suggested replacement for design-r4.txt:559: "The AMX-off compile of the fakes checks only that the packed class definitions are complete at that width; at 8 lanes it is the only such check. The fakes stay where they are to avoid an extra test refactor. A signature drift between a fake and its declaration is caught only by the AMX-on link." (3) Precision in the evidence sentence: the drift surfaces at the AMX-on link as a multiple-definition error, because the pulled-in amx_attn.o also defines available(), begin_region(), end_region() and pack_q_group_128x4() [src/tron/kernels/amx_attn.cpp@2880c3aa9b:76-188], not merely because the object is pulled in. Add CMakeLists.txt@2880c3aa9b:608-611 (no -Wmissing-declarations, no -Werror) to the evidence, since that flag set is what makes a drifted fake compile silently.
Narrow the claim in three places. (1) Replace "gives a benefit that does not exist" with "over-states the benefit". The AMX-off compile of the unconditional fakes does check three things against the header: (a) the by-value parameter classes are complete and instantiate at the fixed 64x128 shape in that build (g++: a definition with an incomplete by-value type is an error, a declaration is not; at 8 lanes the fakes are the only instantiation of the packed V view, because the cache uses the packed owner only in 16-lane blocks [design-r4.txt:389]); (b) the return type and the noexcept specifier agree with the declaration (g++ 11.4 errors: "has a different exception specifier", "ambiguating new declaration"); (c) the alias names and constants the fakes spell exist in the header. (2) Fix the attribution: the quote "checks nothing extra" is from the round-3 G14 design-text lens correction (c) only [r3-findings.md G14]; the source lens said "not a functional loss, because ... a signature mismatch is only observable with AMX on". Same substance, different words. (3) Reword the recommended replacement for design-r4.txt:559 to: "Keep class definitions complete in both vector widths. The AMX-off compile of the unconditional fakes checks only that their parameter classes are complete and that return type and noexcept match the declarations; a parameter-type drift compiles as an unused overload and surfaces only in an AMX-on link, where the static tron library pulls in the real amx_attn.o. The fakes keep their placement to avoid an extra test refactor [Build boundaries]." Add one observation: the section-9 cell carries a rationale that the body [:296] does not; under this round's rule that a Design response cell counts only if the body carries it, the cell should repeat the body's reason (the test-refactor cost) rather than introduce a new one. Citations: design-r4.txt:296, :389, :479-481, :559; self_attention.hpp@2880c3aa9b guarded blocks listed in the reason; src/tron/CMakeLists.txt@2880c3aa9b:187, :219-224; t/t_amx_dispatch_dtype.cpp@2880c3aa9b:39, :166-190, :192-214; t/CMakeLists.txt@2880c3aa9b:594-596 (test registered unconditionally, "Defines CPU fakes for every amx_attn_h128g4 entry point in its own TU"); h/tron/kernels/amx_attn_iface.hpp@2880c3aa9b:219-222, :230-233 (declarations under no preprocessor condition).
Recommendation
In design-r4.txt:559 replace 'This preserves declaration checking by unconditional fake kernels in AMX-off builds.' with 'The AMX-off compile of the fakes checks only class completeness. The fakes stay where they are to avoid an extra test refactor; a signature drift is caught only by the AMX-on link.'

Note L03. Positive kernel-boundary checks must stay unevaluated; AMX-off t_llama_unit links no kernels

Where
8 Behavior checks, Typed function boundaries row, design-r4.txt:465
Earlier ids
touches J29, J39, M03, G30
Claim
The design places the typed-boundary checks in t_llama_unit and asks for positive checks that each kernel accepts its own view. t_llama_unit links tron and pos_fake only (t/CMakeLists.txt@2880c3aa9b:586-592). With TRON_AMX_DISPATCH off, src/tron/kernels/amx_attn.cpp is not compiled, so libtron has no definition of qk_rowmajor_128x4 or weights_times_v_128x4. The declarations in amx_attn_iface.hpp are unconditional (no #if in that header). A positive check written as a real call would compile and then fail to link in the required 16-lane AMX-off configuration. A requires-expression inside STATIC_REQUIRE is unevaluated and does not ODR-use the function, so it links. The design names STATIC_REQUIRE but does not draw this consequence. It also does not say the checks must not run the kernels in the AMX-on build, where a real call outside a tile region would execute AMX code.
Evidence
Design quote and evidence
Positive checks accept each function's own view. Use STATIC_REQUIRE / STATIC_REQUIRE_FALSE for those call contracts
t/CMakeLists.txt@2880c3aa9b:586-592 (t_llama_unit LIBRARIES tron pos_fake); src/tron/CMakeLists.txt@2880c3aa9b:193 (amx_attn.cpp compiled only if TRON_AMX_DISPATCH, facts-r1); h/tron/kernels/amx_attn_iface.hpp@2880c3aa9b:109-114 (includes and namespace, no #if), :219-222 and :230-233 (declarations).
Correction from verification
Narrow the claim: the design's row already forces unevaluated checks (dependent requires constants + STATIC_REQUIRE, design-r4.txt:465; static_assert operands cannot contain a runtime call), so the omission is the missing reason, not a missing constraint. Fix the CMake citation from src/tron/CMakeLists.txt@2880c3aa9b:193 to :219-220 (if(TRON_AMX_DISPATCH) / target_sources(tron PRIVATE kernels/amx_attn.cpp)). Add to the evidence that the only fake definitions live in t_amx_dispatch_dtype's own translation unit (t/t_amx_dispatch_dtype.cpp@2880c3aa9b:166-190; t/CMakeLists.txt@2880c3aa9b:594-596) and that the required Nix tests leave TRON_AMX_DISPATCH off (design-r4.txt:492). Drop the AMX-on tile-region sentence; it is moot once the checks are static. Reword the recommendation for design-r4.txt:465 as: "Write the positive checks as requires-expression constants too, never as evaluated calls: t_llama_unit links tron and pos_fake only (t/CMakeLists.txt:586-592), and libtron holds the kernel definitions only when TRON_AMX_DISPATCH is ON (src/tron/CMakeLists.txt:219-220), which the required Nix tests leave OFF."

Note L04. Owner-to-view construction route and the access helper's member are unnamed

Where
5 Owner and expression reads, design-r4.txt:250; contract table rows at :259-260
Earlier ids
touches J23, J07
Claim
The design forbids any public raw-pointer constructor on views and rows (:259) and requires v_vnni_tensor::as_view() to return a view (:250). No sentence says how as_view() reaches a view constructor that is not public. The reviewer's sketch solves this with friend declarations (the owner and detail::vnni_access are friends of the view) and names the helper's member packed_data. The design names detail::v_vnni_access as a type (:260) but gives no member name or signature, and J23 already records that the word friend was dropped for the helper. The owner side is a second friend that nobody named. An implementer can pick any of three routes (friend owner, a private view constructor taking the owner, or a public constructor from a tagged type). Only the first matches the sketch the reviewer is expected to approve.
Evidence
Design quote and evidence
Owners, views, and rows expose no public raw-pointer constructor, data member, or pointer conversion. Only matrix views construct rows from storage.
Ben's comment 5765866077 in evidence/github-review.json (private ctor from pointer, friend detail::vnni_access::packed_data, as_view() & and const &); design-r4.txt:250, :259, :260; r3-findings.md G01/J23 text on the deleted words ", a friend declared".
Correction from verification
Four narrowings. (1) The ids J23 and J07 appear in no scratchpad file (r1/r2/r3-findings.md, batches/B01-B17.md, facts-r1..r4.md); the report generator should map them to real ids or drop the cross-reference. The fact they stand for is verifiable on its own: design-r3.txt:249 said "a friend declared with the V types" and design-wdiff.txt segment "DELETE @old 3617 / new 4216" removed ", a friend declared" from the row now at design-r4.txt:260. (2) "The word friend was dropped for the helper" is too broad. It was dropped from the contract row [:260], but the design still calls the helpers "the internal friend helpers" in the child test paragraph [design-r4.txt:443]. Say: "the helper's friend status now survives only in one child-test sentence at :443, not in the access contract". (3) The three-route list should become two. A public constructor from a tagged type plus a pointer is a public constructor whose parameter list carries a raw pointer, which the letter of :259 forbids. Route 2 as phrased ("a private view constructor taking the owner") still needs the owner as a friend; rephrase it as "a public view constructor taking the owner by lvalue reference, with the view declared a friend of the owner". The real fork is: owner befriends view (the sketch, line 92) or view befriends owner. Both give identical observable behavior. (4) Extend the recommendation's friend list by the sketch's third friend, because :259 allows writable-to-read-only view conversion and the converting constructor must read the other specialization's private pointer (sketch lines 73-77 and 90-91): "The view's pointer constructor is private. Its friends are v_vnni_tensor<R,D>, v_vnni_view of the other constness (template <typename, size_t, size_t> friend class v_vnni_view;), and detail::v_vnni_access, as in the sketch. detail::v_vnni_access has one static member, template <typename T, size_t R, size_t D> static T* packed_data(v_vnni_view<T,R,D>) noexcept, and nothing else. The child mirrors this for k_vnni_tensor, k_vnni_view, and detail::k_vnni_access." Add to the claim: "This is not a regression: no revision (round-2 text :223, round-3 :249, round-4 :260) named the owner as a friend or the helper's member, and no earlier finding asked for it." Keep severity note and kind omission.
Narrow the finding to an optional note, or drop it. Suggested residual text, if kept: "Section 5 does not restate the construction mechanism that section 2 retains from the sketch [design-r4.txt:79]. The helper detail::v_vnni_access is named at :260 without a member name or signature. 5. The decision record: Q1 to Q4 and D5

Design section 2 carries the decision record: four questions for Ben (Q1 to Q4) with a proposed choice, a fallback if rejected and a record status, plus D5, the native-K typing scope jhan requested. All three judges found the record adequate. The table gives my reading of each row against Ben's three comments (evidence/github-review.json) and jhan's request.

Question or decision, and the proposed choiceCoversAssessment this round
Q1. May each concrete view contain its address formula? Proposed: yes; one view per layout, separate V and K owners, no layout-policy template.Ben's sketch sections 1 and 2 and his reservation: "I'm not entirely convinced of the necessity of separate k_vnni_layout from vnni_view".Adequate and unchanged since round 3. The paragraph under the table now states jhan's reading (the address formula belongs in the view) but no longer says it is right; J04 restores the verdict clause in one sentence.
Q2. Should const matrix and row objects prevent element mutation? Proposed: yes at both ranks.Ben's sketch: "As with std::span, a const view object does not make its elements const. vnni_view<const bf16, Layout> does."Adequate; the departure is flagged with a fallback. The row-conversion clause that round 3 asked for is now in the contract row (rows have no cross-constness conversion, :259).
Q3. May packed views expose logical rows through operator[]? Proposed: yes, a logical row expression with no public pointer; keep bounds-checked at().Ben's review asked for the expr precedent; his sketch excluded operator[] because it suggests a contiguous row.Adequate. The maintainer judge notes one phrase stronger than Ben's words survives at :439 ("the reviewer's uniform-expression requirement"; he wrote "I think the way I would approach this").
Q4. Is the smaller parent scope sufficient? Proposed: yes; expression reads and the existing typed bulk operations; slices, iterators and generic writes deferred.Ben's review: "the usual operations that we expect of tensor types and, in particular, the row-wise load/store operations".Adequate. append_v_row is now mapped to the sketch's V store_row (:266). One departure from the sketch is still stated nowhere: the row operations take tron's const_view and view where the sketch used std::span; the maintainer judge accepts it (the tron view carries the aligned and dma flags) but asks the page to say so, together with the sketch-name to design-name table round 3 requested.

Requested scope: the issue author; reuse the existing K view type through the new const_native_k_view alias.jhan's request and the issue title; not part of Ben's sketch.Right, and the round-3 wording slip is fixed ("the new const_native_k_view alias"). G35 notes that section 2 still alternates between issue author and assignee for the same person.

Nothing in the record misquotes Ben. The maintainer judge re-checked the comment ids and times against the saved JSON (18:24:52; 18:53:47; 18:58:31 edited 18:59:58 UTC) and confirmed that lines 301 and 416 now name his header path h/tron/tensor/vnni.hpp correctly.

6. What the revision gets right

  • design-r4.txt:245 "Declare all five view parameters without defaults" matches the repository's own forward declarations exactly: h/tron/kernels/expr.hpp@2880c3aa9b:17-22 declares view and const_view with the struct key and no Stride default, so kv_cache_fwd.hpp cannot collide with the default at views.hpp:26.
  • design-r4.txt:243 spells the alias stride as std::integer_sequence<ptrdiff_t, Cols, 1>. row_major<seq<Rows,Cols>> is sseq<Cols,1> (seq.hpp@2880c3aa9b:388-394), so const_native_k_view is the same type as today's k_view() return type, and const_view(view const&) at view.hpp:299 is not explicit. The typed QK call at :280 therefore compiles from pg.k(slot, kv_head) (kv_cache.hpp:1549-1569) with no new code.
  • design-r4.txt:262 copies the extraction from the real get_v(float*) loop (kv_cache.hpp:2412-2432, shift-left 16 for even and mask 0xffff0000 for odd) and correctly notes that even_part and odd_part are private members of scaled_v_expr (kv_cache.hpp:1996-2005).
  • design-r4.txt:270 "preserve the existing aligned load/store contract" is accurate: today's set_v uses _mm256_load_epi32 (kv_cache.hpp:2300, 2311, 2335, 2346) and _mm512_load_ps (2371, 2382). The one unaligned source on main is std::array<float,256> at t/heterogeneous_scheduler_compile.cpp:441, which :270 names for alignas(64); today that test passes only by stack placement.
  • design-r4.txt:282 lists the six get_v test callers correctly (t_llama_unit.cpp 819, 1575, 2038, 2539, 2761, 2824 at 2880c3aa9b) and handles the seventh call, the probe at 105-109, in its own sentence. Every one of those destinations is already 64-byte aligned.
  • design-r4.txt:286 gives the two weighted-sum bounds correctly: (count + 1) / 2 * 2 at 16 lanes (kv_cache.hpp:2078) and count at 8 lanes (2139-2175), and states that neither uses the view's static row count.
  • design-r4.txt:366-367 arena rules match the code: the constructor allocates retained then reclaimable and calls refresh_kv_blocks_alias afterwards (kv_cache.hpp:1101-1114); restore does the same (968-971); per slot there are n_kv_heads * n_pages contiguous blocks (1298); arena bytes are n_pages times the sum of n_kv_heads * block_bytes over slots (656-667, 1160-1165), so the bound at :366 divides exactly.
  • design-r4.txt:274 "The existing append path copies token rows, including when a requested range spans a whole page" is right: copy_storage_slot at kv_cache.hpp:1927-1958 copies V token by token through append_v at 16 lanes and by memcpy at 8 lanes, and :285 keeps the 8-lane memcpy unchanged.
  • design-r4.txt:397-403 keep the numerics test's hand-packed vector as expected bytes and its bf16_rne reference. That matches t_amx_numerics.cpp@2880c3aa9b:199-237, where the reference already reads the independent packed vector, so the migrated test keeps an independent oracle.
  • design-r4.txt:451 names the seven changing LoopyTronSpec assertions and the unchanged helper tail at line 3232; the lead verified each line at 30c4ac82cb (facts-r4).
  • design-r4.txt:506-508 require both QK and PV cases to pass without the AMX-unavailable warning and add a TRON_AMX_DISABLE=1 control set before launch; that matches the once-per-process read at src/tron/kernels/amx_attn.cpp:61 and :80.
  • design-r4.txt:514 gives the exact CI lint command (bin/ci/run-lint-checks.sh:7 and :18) and the Note heading convention (bin/lint-notes:21-31), so a moved Note cannot break the lint-toolchain check silently.
  • design-r4.txt:675 (S21): all 31 new links were checked this round by first and last cited line; every range brackets the code its label names. Only the three slips already reported (J44 label, J45 end line, J46 start line) remain.
  • design-r4.txt:296 states the C++ fact that drives the both-widths class-definition rule correctly: a by-value parameter in a function definition needs a complete type, and the fakes at t_amx_dispatch_dtype.cpp:166-190 are unconditional definitions.

8. Verdict

Three judges read the design and the review summary independently. All three give accept with minor edits and say the design is implementable now; all three find the decision record adequate. The implementing agent would build steps 1 to 6 from the page and stop once, at the first test commit, on the CI route (J01). The maintainer judge lists no must-fix and asks for a sketch-name to design-name table and two wording fixes. The risk reviewer checked the numerics, lifetime and concurrency rules against source and found them right; the one consequential gap is CI. The lead's verdict follows: accept with minor edits, implementable now, with the J01 paragraph written before the first test commit and the J09 sentence before step 3.

implementable now		The behavior rules that could silently corrupt data are stated and match source. The NaN table is right: fp32 0x7F800001 truncates to bf16 0x7F80 (+infinity) in the packed append, and the generic conversion gives 0x7F81 (a NaN) only without TRON_IGNORE_NAN, the macro every non-Debug build defines [design-r4.txt:349-352; h/common/numerics/bf16.hpp@f46e48ba:92-109; src/tron/CMakeLists.txt@2880c3aa9b:292-295]. The padding rule is stated as an operation sequence: an even append zeros its odd partner without reading, an odd append merges into an initialized pair, and direct element writes do neither [design-r4.txt:266-267, :319-325]. The inherited bf16_chunk fallback preserves stored bits, because a widened bf16 has a zero low half and fp32s_to_bf16s changes only NaNs with nonzero low bits [design-r4.txt:258; h/tron/kernels/expr.hpp@2880c3aa9b:110-116]. The aligned destination view of load_row adds no fault risk: today's get_v already stores with _mm512_store_ps and _mm256_store_si256, which require alignment, and every existing destination is 64-byte aligned (alignas(64) arrays at t/t_llama_unit.cpp@2880c3aa9b:807, :1563, :2026, :2749, :2812; tensor<head_size> at :2538; the GOF scratch at src/tron/gof.cpp@2880c3aa9b:203) [design-r4.txt:268-270; h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:2421-2431, :2454-2463]. Object lifetime is low risk: the array placement-new form is the one that does not clear storage [design-r4.txt:359-364], the helper runs inside allocate_kv_group after the arena is installed [design-r4.txt:365], and every member it needs (n_pages, support_eagle_, storage_slot_count_) is set in the constructor's initializer list before that call, storage_view_offset_ has a default member initializer, and slot_base returns nullptr for an absent arena [kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:1091-1103, :1129, :1188-1197, :963-973]. The design also demands an inspection of the optimized allocation and restore code for clearing loops [design-r4.txt:372]. Concurrency is unchanged for the parent: pair writes stay sequential and adjust_grains_for_partners stays in model.hpp [design-r4.txt:326-327]; the one-lane-per-line K rule is child scope [design-r4.txt:436]. Process rules are right: stack rule, Skip benchmarks, no Run CI, retarget before pushing the child's new history, cost records under authorization [design-r4.txt:165, :411, :420, :537]. The one consequential gap is CI (J01 with J36). A draft pull request runs only the lint, machinery and fresh-plan jobs; the product pipeline (Build ingest through Test host) needs a non-draft PR or the Run CI label [.github/workflows/gcp-nix.yml@2880c3aa9b:5, :168-171, :294-297, :382-385]. The design requires required-suite execution evidence before committing a new test, forbids the label, and wants per-case Catch2 lines from Slice logs that stay on a runner the implementer usually cannot reach [design-r4.txt:495, :500-504; AGENTS.md@2880c3aa9b:19-24]. It never names the route that satisfies all of these: the parent is the PR based on main and may leave draft [AGENTS.md@2880c3aa9b:12-13; design-r4.txt:165], and the ready_for_review event runs the product pipeline [gcp-nix.yml@2880c3aa9b:13]. The consequence is a stalled PR or a needless exception request, not a wrong build, so this is a text fix and not a redo. What changed since round 3. Round 3 had one design major (G01, where scaled_v_expr obtains the packed address) and two items two judges refused to start without (G22, the numerics fixture route; G09, the constraint mechanism). All three are resolved in the body with every fact checked. The round-3 partly count fell from 25 to 8. This round's one major is a process paragraph the design's own rules require; none of the 47 findings changes what gets built, and the checkers found no regression among the 79 known items. Four minors are wording on the production path or the record (J09, J04, J05, J06); the rest are mechanism sentences a compiler would otherwise surface (L02, J07, J12) or bookkeeping (J15).

What the design does not have to do. It compiled nothing and ran nothing; it posted nothing and edited no issue; sw-dev-01 is codex's user rule; main moving by one line since 2880c3aa9b is covered by its relocate rule. None of those is a finding. Ben's answers to Q1 to Q4 are not needed to start: the design says to implement the proposed choices and label them Proposed in the PR description, and all three judges accept that.

10. Method, evidence, and limits

This round used the round-3 shape. The lead first re-verified the facts the new text relies on (the self_attention.hpp block range, the panel formula against k_vnni::index, the seven LoopyTronSpec assertions, the Slice log path and retention, the lint-toolchain check, the child's 8-lane guard, the three cost-record rows, the fixture lines of t_amx_numerics) against the pinned commits and refreshed the live state of main, issue #4525, PR 4424 and PR 4505; that record is evidence/claude-review-r4/lead-verified-facts-r4.md. Two of the lead's pre-round facts were wrong and are corrected below.

Numbers of this round: 216 agents, 26.3 million tokens, 2 hours 7 minutes; 17 disposition batches (51 agents), 10 finders, 1 merge agent, 46 merged candidates with 138 verifier votes, 1 critic with 4 candidates and 12 votes, 3 judges. The workflow result is evidence/claude-review-r4/workflow-result.json; the inner result (the script's return value) is workflow-result-inner.json.

  • Dispositions: 17 batches of 3 to 6 items; per batch one checker and two re-graders (source-truth, fairness); majority of three; batch files in evidence/claude-review-r4/batches/, built from the round-3 findings by build_inputs.py.
  • New findings: 10 finders (the same ten lenses as round 3, with the regression hunt over the round-3 to round-4 word diff and the section-9 audit over the four disposition tables), plus 16 new defects the checkers found in the fixes; one merge agent deduplicated; three verifiers (source, design-text fairness, intent) per candidate; a finding survived when at least two could not refute it; the lead read every vote before grading and folded the corrections into the recommendations.
  • Completeness critic after verification; its four candidates went through the same three verifiers. The critic also disputed the lead's facts file (see the next bullet) and the severity of J08, which the lead accepted.
  • Lead errors corrected: (1) lead-verified-facts-r4.md called the design's sentence at :270 defensible on the strength of the round-3 G41 lead note (dma = false); the production V buffer is hardware::btensor, a dmatensor with dma = true, so the sentence is wrong (J09) and the facts file now carries a correction block. (2) The same file named t_llama_unit.cpp:819 and :2038 as the bf16 get_v callers; the bf16 callers are :819 and :1575, and :2038 is a float caller (J37). (3) The round-3 facts file gave the self_attention.hpp width-check block as 31-37; it is 30-36, as the design says.
  • Three judges (implementer, maintainer, risk) read the design and the review summary independently.
  • Limits: no code was compiled or run; no chromium on this host, so the page was checked structurally (ASCII, anchors, links) and not rendered; the Test host runner measurement (J36) is a read-only GitHub API sample of the most recent runs, not a full history; agents can make mistakes, so every finding carries its evidence and votes; the lead's own reading is not a substitute for Ben's.
  • Evidence: evidence/claude-review-r4/ holds the workflow result, findings.json, build_inputs.py, build_findings.py, gen_review.py, overrides-static.json, make_overrides.py, overrides.json, the design text and word diff, the link table, the facts file and the batch files. The round-3 pages are archived as claude-review-design-new-tensor-type-r3.html and -r3-votes.html.
